## Document 003: 10月企業向けサービス価格指数 ～前年比＋4.2％、国際運輸以外でも伸びが拡大～

Source PDF: all report pdf/10月企業向けサービス価格指数 ～前年比＋4.2％、国際運輸以外でも伸びが拡大～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-11-25
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Economic Indicators

2026年 11月 25日

1/2 
10月企業向けサービス価格指数 
～前年比＋4.2％、国際運輸以外でも伸びが拡大～ 
 経済調査部 主席エコノミスト  阿原 健一郎 
（℡：050-5470-4868）

1. 10月の企業向けサービス価格指数は前年比＋4.2％に加速 
 本日公表された 10月の企業向けサービス価格指数は前年比＋4.2％と、前月（同＋3.8％）から
伸びが加速した。内訳を見ると、「運輸・郵便」の前年比寄与度が＋1.53％ptとなり、引き続き総
平均を押し上げている（図表１）。なかでも「外航貨物輸送」は前年比＋120.4％と、前月（同＋
97.6％）から上昇幅が一段と拡大した。 
 また、「広告」の下落幅が前年比▲1.2％と、前月（同▲5.3％）から縮小したことも、総平均の
伸びを押し上げた。特にテレビ・ラジオ広告の下落幅縮小が大きく、広告全体では総平均の前年
比の伸びを前月から＋0.18％pt押し上げている。このほか、宿泊サービスや廃棄物処理、自動車
整備で上昇幅が拡大し、「諸サービス」も前年比＋3.1％と、前月（同＋2.8％）から伸びが高まっ
た。今月の加速には、国際運輸の価格上昇に加え、国内サービスの価格変動も寄与している。

２. 国際運輸を除いても伸びが拡大、高人件費率サービスは底堅く推移 
 海外市況の影響を受けやすい国際運輸を除いた「企業向けサービス価格指数（除く国際運輸）」
は前年比＋3.2％と、前月（同＋3.0％）から伸びが高まった（図表２）。外航貨物輸送や広告など
を含む「低人件費率サービス」は同＋5.9％と、前月（同＋5.0％）から伸びが大きく拡大してお
り、総平均の加速を主導している。 
 一方、人件費の影響を受けやすい「高人件費率サービス」は前年比＋2.7％と、前月（同＋
2.6％）から小幅に伸びが高まった。前年比＋２％台後半の上昇が続いており、人件費増加を価格
に転嫁する動きは維持されているとみられる。もっとも、労働者派遣サービスなどで伸びが高ま
る一方、ソフトウェア開発や警備などでは鈍化しており、品目ごとの動きにはばらつきがある。
総平均の加速に比べて高人件費率サービスの伸びの拡大は小幅にとどまっていることから、賃金
を起点とする価格上昇が一段と強まったとまでは評価しにくい。今後も、人件費の価格転嫁が幅
広い分野で継続し、サービス価格の持続的な上昇につながるかを注視する必要がある。

以 上

## Document 056: 2026年10月宏观数据点评：生产需求均有回落

Report source: 上海证券
Date: 2026-11-26
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

生产需求均有回落
——2026 年 10 月宏观数据点评
[Table_Industry]日期: shzqdatemark [Table_Summary] 主要观点
生产需求均有回落
作为经济成效综合体现的工业生产增速有所放缓。三大门类产业增速
均有所下滑。10 月主要行业中食品、医药、电子设备、有色金属冶炼
有所回升；纺织、专用设备、汽车、其他运输设备、黑色金属冶炼有
所下降，其中汽车下降幅度较大。
固定投资增速上月企稳后，跌幅再度扩大。分项来看，制造业投资下
降 2.9%，基础设施投资同比下降 5.1%，跌幅继续扩大。下阶段，要
统筹好各类政府投资基金，加快重大项目开工落地，持续激发民间投
资活力，推动扩大有效投资，促进高质量发展。房地产开发投资降幅
仍在扩大。分项来看，地产销售面积跌幅扩大；供给端施工面积跌幅
扩大，新开工面积跌幅小幅收窄。从房企开发资金的来源来看，房地
产资金跌幅有所扩大，分项中除个人按揭项外跌幅仍在扩大。地产投
资仍待企稳。

10 月份，社会消费品零售总额 46463 亿元，同比增长 0.4%。具体来
看，各品类消费除家具外均有所下降，通讯器材跌幅较大汽车消费仍
偏低迷。10 月商品零售、餐饮消费增速均有所下滑。
加力扩大内需，优化供给
二季度以来，需求放缓，投资持续负增，消费表现偏软。二季度经济
增速较一季度有所回落，但三季度反弹至 4.5%。前三季度 4.6%的经
济增速也为完成全年目标打下了较好的基础。10 月生产需求均有回
落。政策方面，政治局会议提出实施好更加积极的财政政策和适度宽
松的货币政策，充分发挥各项存量政策效能，及时谋划出台务实管用
的增量政策，加大逆周期调节力度。针对需求方面，会议指出要有效
扩大国内需求，适应不同群体消费需求扩大优质供给，挖掘服务消费
潜力；扎实推进“六张网”规划建设。我们认为在政策助力下，加力
扩大内需、优化供给，推动经济持续向新向优向好发展，努力实现
“十五五”良好开局。
 风险提示
地缘政治事件恶化，国际金融形势改变，中美政策超预期变化。
[Table_Author]分析师: 陈彦利

1 事件：10 月份宏观数据公布
工业生产放缓。10 月份，规模以上工业增加值同比实际增长
4.8%（增加值增速均为扣除价格因素的实际增长率）。从环比看，
10 月份，规模以上工业增加值比上月增长 0.21%。1—10 月份，规
模以上工业增加值同比增长 5.3%。
固定资产跌幅扩大。1—10 月份，全国固定资产投资（不含农
户）364824 亿元，同比下降 8.1%。其中，知识产权产品投资同比
增长 9.2%
消费再度回落。1—10 月份，社会消费品零售总额 416464 亿
元，同比增长 1.0%。其中，除汽车以外的消费品零售额 383811 亿
元，增长 2.8%。10 月份，社会消费品零售总额 46463 亿元，同比
增长 0.4%。其中，除汽车以外的消费品零售额 43162 亿元，增长
2.5%。
表 1：本期经济数据增速（%）
指标 本期值 前值
工业增加值（当月） 4.8 5.5
固定资产投资（不含农户，累计） -8.1 -7.2
社会消费品零售（当月） 0.4 1.1

资料来源：统计局，IFIND，上海证券研究所
2 事件解析：数据特征和变动原因
2.1 工业生产有所放缓
10 月工业生产有所放缓，同比增速下降 0.7 个百分点。分三
大门类看，10 月份，采矿业增加值同比增长 0.7%，制造业增长
5.4%，电力、热力、燃气及水生产和供应业增长 4.4%。三大门类
产业增速均有所下滑。从产量的增速来看，原煤、原油产量小幅
上升；制造业方面，焦炭、水泥、工业机器人、集成电路产量有
所回升；公共事业方面，发电量有所下降、天然气产量有所回升。
另外出口交货值有所回升。

输设备、黑色金属冶炼有所下降，其中汽车下降幅度较大。

2.3 固定投资跌幅持续加深

同比下降 8.1%，跌幅进一步加深。其中，第一产业投资 7018 亿
元，同比下降 6.1%；第二产业投资 142333 亿元，下降 3.6%；第
三产业投资 215473 亿元，下降 10.9%。三大产业累计投资增速全
面回落。
固定投资增速上月企稳后，跌幅再度扩大。分项来看，制造
业投资下降 2.9%，基础设施投资同比下降 5.1%，跌幅继续扩大。
下阶段，要统筹好各类政府投资基金，加快重大项目开工落地，
持续激发民间投资活力，推动扩大有效投资，促进高质量发展。

2.4 地产投资仍是拖累
1—10 月份，全国房地产开发投资 57215 亿元，同比下降
21.6%，降幅仍在扩大。分项来看，地产销售面积跌幅扩大；供给
端施工面积跌幅扩大，新开工面积跌幅小幅收窄。从房企开发资
金的来源来看，房地产资金跌幅有所扩大，分项中除个人按揭项
外跌幅仍在扩大。地产投资仍待企稳。

2.5 消费小幅回落
10 月份，社会消费品零售总额 46463 亿元，同比增长 0.4%。

费品零售额增长 1.5%。城乡消费双双回落。

有色金属冶炼有所回升；纺织、专用设备、汽车、其他运输设备、
黑色金属冶炼有所下降，其中汽车下降幅度较大。

业投资下降 2.9%，基础设施投资同比下降 5.1%，跌幅继续扩大。
下阶段，要统筹好各类政府投资基金，加快重大项目开工落地，
持续激发民间投资活力，推动扩大有效投资，促进高质量发展。

4 事件预测：趋势判断
4.1 加力扩大内需，优化供给

### Page 8

宏观数据
请务必阅读尾页重要声明 8
二季度以来，需求放缓，投资持续负增，消费表现偏软。二
季度经济增速较一季度有所回落，但三季度反弹至 4.5%。前三季
度 4.6%的经济增速也为完成全年目标打下了较好的基础。10 月生
产需求均有回落。政策方面，政治局会议提出实施好更加积极的
财政政策和适度宽松的货币政策，充分发挥各项存量政策效能，
及时谋划出台务实管用的增量政策，加大逆周期调节力度。针对
需求方面，会议指出要有效扩大国内需求，适应不同群体消费需
求扩大优质供给，挖掘服务消费潜力；扎实推进“六张网”规划
建设。我们认为在政策助力下，加力扩大内需、优化供给，推动
经济持续向新向优向好发展，努力实现“十五五”良好开局。

美國消費動能維持韌性、房市復甦仍受壓抑，通膨風險與 AI 金融隱憂牽動聯
準會政策走向 
經濟 
⚫ 10 月美國零售銷售月增 0.2%、年增 6.8%，近三個月累計年增 6.4%；核
心零售月增 0.5%，消費支出仍具韌性。惟整體增幅放緩，後續須關注通
膨、利率及就業情勢變化。 
⚫ 11 月美國消費者信心指數由 48.0 升至 51.5，月增 7.3%，反映選後景氣
預期改善及汽油價格回落；短期與長期通膨預期分別降至 3.7%及 3.3%，
惟生活成本壓力仍存，整體信心仍偏弱。 
⚫ 10 月美國建築許可年化總數降至 129.5 萬戶，月減 7.4%；惟新屋開工
增至 130.9 萬戶，月增 9.9%，多戶住宅開工大幅回升。完工戶數月增
6.5%，顯示住宅供給持續釋出，惟高房貸利率仍抑制建商投資意願。 
⚫ 10 月 FOMC 會議紀要顯示，美國經濟與勞動市場維持韌性，AI 投資持
續帶動經濟活動；惟 9 月 PCE 及核心 PCE 通膨率升至 3.6%及 3.1%，
通膨上行風險仍存。多數官員支持維持利率於 3.75%至 4.00%，但認為
未來仍可能進一步升息，並關切 AI 相關融資及金融穩定風險。 
重要 
事件

中國取得海外算力，顯示管制重心由晶片延伸至運算能力，恐提高中國
AI 發展成本並加劇美中科技競爭。 
⚫ 輝達代理執行長 Colette Kress 表示，未來將不再向中國銷售 Hopper 系
列 AI 晶片，包括 H200 產品，且後續財測已排除相關營收；在美國持
續收緊 AI 晶片出口管制下，市場憂心恐衝擊資料中心業務表現，惟公
司認為先進 AI 晶片需求強勁，整體成長動能仍穩健。 
⚫ 美國總統川普、中國國家主席習近平及俄羅斯總統普丁於深圳舉行歷史
性三邊峰會，就全球安全、貿易、能源及科技治理等議題交換意見。外
界認為，此次會晤反映大國溝通協調需求升高，對未來國際政治經濟秩
序及地緣局勢發展具有重要指標意義。

國際經濟金融週報 
兆豐國際商業銀行 徵信處 
115.11.23 
一、 美國—消費動能維持韌性、房市復甦仍受壓抑，通膨風險與 AI 金融隱憂牽
動聯準會政策走向 
2026 年 10 月美國零售與餐飲服務銷售額經季節調整後為 7,482 億美元，較
上月增加 0.2%，年增 6.8%；近三月(8-10 月)累計銷售額年增 6.4%，消費支出仍
具支撐。若扣除汽車及零件銷售，零售額月增 0.6%；進一步扣除汽車、零件及加
油站後，核心零售銷售月增 0.5%，與前月持平。主要業別中，雜項零售商、非實
體零售商、加油站及健康與個人護理用品商店銷售成長較佳，餐飲服務亦維持正
成長；惟汽車及零件經銷商、家具及家居用品店與服飾配件店銷售下滑。整體而
言，10 月零售銷售增幅雖較上月放緩，但核心零售及服務消費表現穩健，顯示美
國內需仍具支撐力道，後續仍須關注通膨、利率及就業情勢變化對消費動能之影
響。 
密西根大學公布，2026 年 11 月美國消費者信心指數終值由上月的 48.0 回升
至 51.5，月增 7.3%、年增 1.0%，惟仍較今年 2 月美伊衝突爆發前低約 9%，顯示
消費者信心雖有所改善，但整體仍處低檔。分項來看，當前經濟狀況指數由 46.7
升至 49.4，月增 5.8%、年減 3.3%；消費者預期指數則由 48.8 升至 52.9，月增
8.4%、年增 3.7%，反映民眾對未來經濟前景的看法有所改善。調查指出，期中選
舉後，消費者預期未來分裂政府格局可能帶來政策調整，推升短期及長期企業景
氣預期，其中獨立選民改善最為明顯，民主黨支持者亦有所回升，惟共和黨支持
者的預期則轉弱。此外，汽油價格下降使低資產及部分中等資產家庭的財務狀況
改善，但持股較多的家庭對個人財務狀況的評價則有所下降，與 AI 相關因素引
發的股市波動相呼應。通膨預期方面，未來一年通膨預期由 4.7%大幅降至 3.7%，
惟仍高於今年 2 月的 3.4%；長期通膨預期亦由 3.5%降至 3.3%，仍高於 2024 年
2.8%至 3.2%的區間，顯示通膨疑慮雖有所緩解，但尚未完全消除。 
2026 年 10 月美國住宅市場建築許可年化總數為 129.5 萬戶，較上月減少 7.4%、
較去年同期減少 8.7%，其中單戶住宅建築許可降至 77.2 萬戶，月減 8.7%；5 戶
以上多戶住宅建築許可則降至 47.5 萬戶，月減 5.2%，年減 0.4%，住宅開發規劃
仍趨審慎。另一方面，新屋開工年化數增至 130.9 萬戶，月增 9.9%、年增 2.8%，

其中單戶住宅開工增至 86.4 萬戶，月增 4.0%；5 戶以上多戶住宅開工升至 43.3
萬戶，月增 25.5%，帶動整體開工表現回升。施工方面，在建住宅年化數微降至
127.2 萬戶，月減 0.1%、年減 3.0%；完工年化數則升至 127.2 萬戶，月增 6.5%，
其中單戶住宅完工增至 90.0 萬戶，月增 6.3%，惟整體仍較去年同期減少 10.4%。
整體而言，美國 10 月住宅市場需求仍受高房貸利率及住房負擔能力壓力影響，
建築許可續呈下滑，惟新屋開工及完工表現改善，顯示住宅供給仍持續釋出，後
續仍須關注房貸利率走勢、住宅需求及營建成本變化對房市發展之影響。 
2026 年 10 月 FOMC 會議紀要重點如下：(1)第三季 GDP 成長動能增強，高
科技產品進口及 AI 相關投資維持熱絡；(2)勞動市場整體穩定並接近充分就業狀
態，失業率維持於 4.2%，薪資成長較前一年放緩；(3)9 月 PCE 及核心 PCE 通膨
率分別升至 3.6%及 3.1%，能源價格上漲、先前關稅措施及 AI 投資帶動之需求與
供給壓力，持續使通膨高於 2%目標，多數官員認為短期內上行風險仍偏高；(4)
多數官員支持將聯邦基金利率目標區間維持於 3.75%至 4.00%，以評估前次升息
及金融條件變化對經濟之影響，惟幾乎所有官員均認為未來仍可能進一步升息，
以確保通膨回落至目標水準；(5)官員普遍關切 AI 相關債務快速擴張、複雜融資
及循環融資安排可能引發之估值修正風險，並持續關注避險基金槓桿、銀行對非
銀行金融機構之間接曝險，以及網路安全等潛在金融穩定議題。 
美國川普政府於 11 月 16 日宣布進一步收緊中國取得輝達（Nvidia）等企業
先進人工智慧（AI）晶片的管道，將新加坡、泰國、馬來西亞、印尼及巴西列為
高風險轉運國並要求出口許可，同時限制向中國企業提供大量算力的海外資料中
心取得受管制晶片。此舉正值美國總統川普前往亞洲、並於 11 月 19 日在深圳與
中國國家主席習近平會晤前後，凸顯華府在防堵晶片轉運與遠端算力租用漏洞上
的政策動向。相較過去主要限制實體晶片出口，新措施進一步強化對海外資料中
心及 AI 算力使用的監管，反映美國持續將管制重點由硬體設備延伸至運算能力
本身，以限制中國取得高階 AI 模型訓練與推理所需資源。市場預期短期內恐提
高中國科技企業取得先進算力的成本與難度，並加速中國發展國產 AI 晶片及自
主算力基礎設施；另一方面，美中科技競爭進一步升溫，後續相關出口管制範圍、
執行細節及北京的反制措施，將持續牽動全球半導體、雲端服務及 AI 產業供應
鏈布局。 
輝達代理執行長 Colette Kress 於法說會表示，公司未來將不再向中國銷售經

美國政府核准出口的 Hopper 系列資料中心產品，包括 H200 人工智慧晶片，此舉
被視為輝達對中國市場策略的重要轉變。輝達第三季仍向中國銷售約 24 億美元
Hopper 產品，但未來財測已不再納入相關出貨收入；同時，公司宣布成立董事會
層級委員會，負責監督國家安全政策、出口管制合規及全球貿易風險。在美國近
期進一步強化對中國 AI 晶片及跨境算力服務管制之際，市場關注新措施對輝達
營運的影響。投資銀行 Jefferies 估計，新規可能使輝達明年資料中心業務營收減
少 440 億至 620 億美元；不過公司認為，受惠於 Vera Rubin 等先進 AI 晶片需求
強勁，整體營收成長動能仍可望維持穩健。 
美國總統川普、中國國家主席習近平及俄羅斯總統普丁於深圳舉行歷史性三
邊高峰會，成為近年國際政治最受矚目的外交活動之一。此次會談聚焦全球安全
情勢、經貿合作、能源供應、AI 治理、科技競爭及區域衝突等重大議題，並就俄
烏戰爭、中東局勢、美中貿易關係、半導體出口管制及全球供應鏈穩定等議題交
換意見。市場尤其關注美中雙方是否於關稅措施、科技管制及投資限制等爭議領
域釋出緩和訊號，以及美俄是否就地緣安全與能源市場穩定達成進一步共識。分
析人士指出，本次峰會反映主要大國透過高層直接對話管控分歧、降低衝突風險
的意願升高，亦凸顯全球治理架構正朝多極化方向發展。儘管三方在烏克蘭戰爭、
台海安全、軍事部署及高科技競爭等核心議題上仍存在明顯立場差異，短期內達
成實質突破的難度仍高，惟此次會晤有助維持主要大國溝通管道，並為後續經貿、
能源及安全事務協調奠定基礎。峰會成果及相關聯合聲明內容，將持續受到國際
社會、金融市場及企業界高度關注，其後續發展亦可能對全球政治經濟秩序產生
深遠影響。 
二、歐元區—工業生產、營造表現皆暫時回溫 
 歐元區 9 月工業生產月增 0.2%，中止連續三個月的衰退，主要是車廠開始回
補汽車庫存，帶動汽車生產反彈，使耐久消費財產出月增 1.3%，抵銷因零售需求
降溫，導致能源(包含燃油等)、非耐久消費財產出月減 1.7%、0.4%的影響。另外，
9 月營造業產出回升 0.1%，中止連續四個月的衰退局面，不過，營造業持續面臨
能源與建材成本上漲，加上法國與西班牙等核心經濟體的房建與公共預算受限，
使營造業的疲軟局面暫時難以扭轉。 
 儘管歐元區 9 月工業與營造業產出止跌回升，但復甦基礎仍受到國際政經不

確定性影響。根據歐盟執委會 2026 年秋季經濟預測，歐元區 2026 與 2027 年實

出等逆風下，內需恐將持續呈壓。 
三、日本—出口持續改善，通膨壓力強化升息預期 
 9 月核心機械訂單(扣除波動訂單)月減 0.3%，第三季核心機械訂單季增 1.2%，
連續第四季成長，顯示企業資本支出動能仍然穩健。其中製造業是主要成長引擎，
AI 與半導體需求正逐步朝更廣泛的製造業擴散，化學產品及商用機械訂單分別成
長 57.0%及 80.5%，反映半導體材料及設備需求依然旺盛。相較之下，非製造業
資本支出動能較為溫和，反映成本上升導致企業調整部分投資計畫，惟勞動市場
結構性問題仍將支撐自動化與生產力相關投資。整體而言，日本資本支出週期尚
未出現明顯轉弱跡象，在 AI 與半導體投資外溢效應帶動下，預期資本支出仍可
維持穩健成長。 
 10 月出口年增 20.5%，其中汽車出口成長 20.0%，成為主要推動力，反映先
前災害造成的供應中斷逐步消退；半導體相關出口成長 49.7%，對中國以外的亞
洲市場科技產品出口更創歷史新高，顯示 AI 與資本支出相關需求持續強勁。從
地區來看，對歐盟、美國及中國出口均成長，其中對美出口已回升至 2025 年 2 月
以來高點。整體而言，出口動能持續改善，汽車出口復甦、AI、資本支出相關需
求可望成為後續出口的重要支撐。 
 10 月全國消費者物價指數(CPI)年增 2.3%，顯示通膨動能持續增強，主要受到
石油產品價格上漲持續轉嫁至商品價格，且漲價壓力已由商品逐步擴散至食品及
服務。雖然政府釋出大米儲備、高中免學費及能源補貼等措施壓低部分價格，使
米價及學費明顯下跌，但剔除生鮮食品後的核心通膨仍達 2.2%，高於央行 2%的
通膨目標。展望未來，隨汽油補貼自 12 月起逐步縮減，加上食品業者可能於 2027
年 4 月消費稅減免生效前提前調漲價格，預期 2027 年通膨壓力將進一步升高，
進而促使日本央行持續升息。

受到出口維持高度成長帶動，10 月工業生產年增率較 9 月下滑 0.7 個百分點
至 4.8%(累計 1-10 月工業生產年增率持平 1-9 月水準為 5.3%)。依產業別來看，
10 月主要受到化工、化纖、黑色金屬冶煉及加工、汽車、手機生產減少影響，主
因國慶長假後過，部分產業向下調節庫存，加上手機銷售狀況不如預期、民眾購
車意願減弱等。不過，IC、機器人生產表現加速擴張，反映資料中心建置及政府
扶植機器人產業等持續強化相關產業表現。 
展望未來，房地產的調整週期可能會延續至 2028 年後，加上去產能政策與供
應鏈外移壓抑民間投資，消費與投資動能料將維持疲軟。在出口方面，近期 APEC
峰會促成中加、中澳經貿破冰並推進亞太自貿區，有助支撐出口表現。然而，美
中貿易關係在川習會後依然沒有重大突破，明年一月美中關稅休戰期滿，仍為貿
易環境帶來不確定因素。 
五、印度及東南亞—泰國 2026 年第三季 GDP2.9%；印尼央行升息以穩定匯價 
(1)新加坡：10 月非石油國內出口（NODX）年增 79.8%，連續第 24 個月成長並
創史上最大增幅，其中，人工智慧相關需求的強勁成長，持續帶動電子產品出口
成長 73.9%，以 磁碟媒體產品（189.6%）、個人電腦（81.0%）和積體電路（53.1%）
成長最顯著；另在船舶(因向巴西石油公司交付海上浮式生產儲卸油船)、藥品
（253.1%）和專用機械（57.3%）帶動下，非電子產品出口大增 81.9%。對美國和
中國的出口分別激增 214.6%和 144.9%，其次是歐盟（90.8%）、台灣（79.3%）
和香港（65.4%），但對印度（-35.8%）和印尼（-19.0%）的出口有所下降。整體
而言，新加坡出口雖繳出亮麗成績單，惟隨美國選後 AI 基礎建設可能放緩，加
上近期因輝達黃仁勳桃色風波，美國商務部將新加坡列入晶片出口管制國家，恐
影響後續出口表現。

(2)印尼：印尼央行利率會議意外升息 1 碼(BI rate)至 6%，貸款利率和隔夜存款利
率分別調高 1 碼至 6.75%和 5.25%，旨在全球經濟情勢也充滿不確定性、印尼貨
幣面臨新一輪壓力下，採取先發制人動作，力求匯率穩定並使通膨率控制在
2.5%±1%的目標區間內，央行同時維持對 2026 年 GDP 成長 4.9%至 5.7%的預測。
展望未來，維持外匯穩定仍是印尼央行的首要政策重點，在通膨居高不下、美國
聯準會升息、美元走強，資金外流壓力仍高下，未來仍有進一步升息風險。 
(3)泰國：2026 年第三季經濟成長率(GDP)年增 2.9%，主要得益於全球對人工智慧
相關產品的強勁需求，帶動出口大幅成長 18.3%，同時，進口成長放緩至 16.3%，
放大淨出口對經濟的貢獻；私人消費（2.5%）和固定投資（11.8%）成長速度加
快，惟政府支出四個季度以來首次下降（-1.6%）。按產業別來看，製造業產出成
長 3.7%為主要貢獻來源、運輸和倉儲業（3.1%）、批發和零售業（6.0%）的產出
成長加速，但旅遊業依然疲軟。泰國政府將今年的 GDP 成長預期從先前的 2.0%-
2.5%上調至 2.5%，高於 2025 年的 2.4%。 
(4)馬來西亞：10 月出口額年增 61.1%，達 MYR 2,392 億元的歷史新高，持續反
映 AI 帶動的強勁需求，電子電氣產品出口增幅達 87.2%，礦產品出口亦大幅成長
37.0%，抵銷農產品出口下降 6.1%的影響，對美國、台灣、新加坡出口分別激增
108%、293%、55%。10 月進口額年增 54.4%至 MYR 1,974 億元，其中，中間產
品（64.0%）、資本貨物（32.9%）、消費品（11.8%）等進口均有成長，按產業別
觀之，製造業、礦業進口額分別成長 60.6%、44.2%，本月自印度（119.6%）、韓
國（194.0%）進口呈倍數成長，自中國、東協進口亦成長 56.5%、64.8%。展望未
來，美國商務部 11/16 發布新規，將新加坡、馬來西亞、泰國、印尼及巴西等五
國列為晶片轉運高風險國家，要求先進 AI 晶片出口至上述國家的部分數據中心
須事先取得許可，並進一步限制中國企業透過海外數據中心遠端租用算力。由於
馬來西亞資料中心容量多屬「共置（colocation）」型態，中國企業占比逾半數，
一旦中國企業難以透過馬國及周邊國家租用高階算力，將影響中國企業投資意
願，加上合規成本增加，將影響未來出口及經濟表現。

1.預估 4Q / 2026 單季稅後 EPS + 2.34 元，創最近 67 季新高 
裕民公告 2026 年 10 月 單月營收 YoY + 51%，創歷史新高。 
宏遠基於保守原則，估裕民 11 月營收 18.50 億元； 12 月營
收 19.05 億元； 估 4Q 單季稅後 EPS + 2.34 元，YoY + 51%。 
若裕民 11 月 及 12 月營收 高於預期，則須上修 4Q 獲利。 
2.BDI 處於獲利水準，裕民 2027 年增加 6 艘新船，將受惠 
2026 年 前三季 BDI 平均 2,586；裕民前三季稅後 EPS 5.80
元。 2026 年 11 月 13 日 BDI 2,980，仍處於明顯獲利水準。 
裕民 2027 年新增 6艘船：輕便型 3艘 + 海岬船 2艘 + 1 艘 
高階海事工程船（用於風力發電工程）； 裕民總船數從 2026
年 72 艘，增加至 2027 年底 78 艘。 
3.海運機構估計 2026 年供給過剩，實際上，BDI 大漲 64% 
海運機構預期 2026 年散裝航運供給過剩，但 2026 年（ 截 至 11
月 13 日）BDI 運價指數平均 2,615，遠高於 2025 年同期平均
值 1,597，高出 64%； 很明顯，海運機構的預測值，忽略某些
關鍵性因素，導致預測結果出現偏差。 
目前海運研究機構 BIMCO，預估 2027 年散裝航運供給成長率
（ + 3.5% ~ + 4.5%），將超過需求成長率（ + 0.5% ~ + 
1.5% ），延續 2026 年供給過剩的看法。 
宏遠基於保守原則，暫時採用 BIMCO 看法： 
(1)假設 2027 年 BDI 運價下跌，裕民 2027 年毛利率 + 28.8%，
將明顯低於 2026 年毛利率 + 35.3%，下滑 6.5 個百分點。 
(2)裕民依照造船計畫，2027 年新增 6 艘新船。 
宏遠估計裕民 2027 年稅後 EPS + 6.51 元，將低於 2026 年預
估值 + 8.14 元； 若 2027 年毛利率下滑幅度，低於 6.5 個百
分點，則必須上調裕民 2027 年獲利預估值。

貳、產 業 及 營 運 概 況 
一.公司簡介 
截至 2026 年 9 月（法說資料），裕民實際經營船隻為 72 艘： 
海岬型船  25 艘：自有 22 艘 + 3 艘合資。 
巴拿馬型船 24 艘：自有 14 艘 + 7 艘代管 + 3 艘合資。 
輕便型     8艘（自有）。 
水泥船     4艘（自有）。 
油輪       4艘：透過 環能海運（持股 40%）。 
CTV 離岸風電人員運輸船 6 艘（自有 ； 裕民風能航運）。 
天然氣船   1 艘（持有 50%；與日本 K LINE 合資）。

在建船隻 14 艘，交船時程： 
2026 年：輕便型 1 艘（預期 11 月交付）。 
2027 年：輕便型 3 艘+ 海岬船 2 艘+ 高階海事工程船 1 艘。 
2028 年：輕便型 2 艘+ 海岬船 2 艘+ 高階海事工程船 1 艘。 
2030 年：海岬船 2 艘。

二.產業趨勢概況 
1.海運機構預期 2026 年供給過剩； 實際上，BDI 走勢強勁 
雖然，先前，多家海運專業研調機構，都預測 2026 年全球
散裝航運，供給成長率超過需求成長率，將出現供給過剩的
狀況。

實際上，2026 年截至 11 月 13 日，散裝 BDI 運價走勢都優於
2025 年同期表現（參閱圖 1） 。

受惠於 BDI 的強勁走勢，裕民： 
1Q / 2026 稅後 EPS + 1.17 元，優於 1Q / 2025 同期 + 0.27 元。 
2Q / 2026 稅後 EPS + 2.34 元，優於 2Q / 2025 同期 + 0.77 元。 
3Q / 2026 稅後 EPS + 2.29 元，優於 3Q / 2025 同期 + 1.72 元。 
裕民公告 2026 年 10 月 單月營收 創歷史新高。

從 BDI 的表現來看： 
1Q / 2026（過年淡季）BDI 平均 1,955。 
2Q / 2026（美伊戰爭爆發，燃煤替代天然氣）BDI 平均 2,751。 
3Q / 2026  BDI 平均 3,041。 
2026 年 11 月 13 日 單日 BDI  2,980。 
2025 年 4Q 旺季 BDI 高點 2,845。

4Q 是散裝航運的季節性旺季，遇上， 荷姆斯海峽封閉 推升 
煤炭的海運需求  及   巴拿馬運河缺水 降低 海運船舶供
給（繞道等同減少供給）； 預期 4Q BDI 的 YoY 表現，將會
持續強勁。

BDI 的 YoY 漲勢強勁，宏遠預期裕民 4Q / 2026 單季獲利 YoY + 
51% ； 未來，4Q / 2026 財報發布，將激勵股價表現。

叁、業 績 及 獲 利 預 估 
1.估 2026 年稅後 EPS + 8.14 元，YoY + 89%

先前，多家國際海運研調機構，預期 2026 年散裝航運供給過
剩，但 2026 年（截至 11 月 13 日）BDI 運價指數平均 2,615，
遠高於 2025 年同期平均值 1,597，高 出 64%（參閱圖 1）； 很
明顯，海運機構的預測值，忽略某些關鍵性因素，導致預測結
果出現偏差。

裕民公告 1Q / 2026 財報營收 38.92 億元，YoY + 13%。 
稅後 EPS + 1.17 元，YoY + 335%。 
公告 2Q / 2026 財報營收 50.14 億元，YoY + 39%。 
稅後 EPS + 2.34 元，YoY + 204%。 
公告 3Q / 2026 財報營收 52.78 億元，YoY + 23%。 
稅後 EPS + 2.29 元，YoY + 33%。 
公告 2026 年 10 月單月營收 21.77 億元，YoY + 57%，新高。

裕民 2026 年 11 月 將下水 1艘 輕便型船。

宏遠估計，裕民 2026 年： 
營收 201.16 億元，YoY + 23%；

營業利益 61.96 億元，YoY + 67%；

業外淨收支 + 8.43 億元，YoY + 503%； 
權益法轉投資收益改善 及 金融資產評價利益增加。

稅後純益 68.75 億元，YoY + 89%；

稅後 EPS + 8.14 元，優於 2025 年財報稅後 EPS + 4.31 元。

預估 裕民  
2026 年稅後 EPS 預估值 
8.14 元 
創最近 18 年新高 
（2009 年 ~ 2026 年）

2.估 2027 年稅後 EPS + 6.51 元，YoY - 20% 
假設：(1) 海運研調機構預測， 2027 年全球散裝航運過剩。 
2027 年 BDI 下跌。 
截至目前（參閱圖 1），BDI 的 YoY 漲勢仍是強勁的。 
(2) 假設 BDI 下跌，裕民 2027 年毛利率下滑 6.5 個百分點。 
(3) 裕民依照訂船規劃，2027 年新增 6艘船舶。

宏遠估計，裕民 2027 年： 
營收 220.56 億元，YoY + 10%； 
2026 年 11 月 下水 1艘新船； 
2027 年 下水 6艘新船； 
成為 2027 年營收上升的主因。

營業利益 54.17 億元，YoY - 13%； 
BDI 下跌，造成 毛利率 及 營利率 下滑。

業外淨收支 + 3.59 億元，YoY - 57%； 
2026 年認列金融資產評價利益。 
2027 年配合新船下水，利息費用上升。

稅後純益 55.04 億元，YoY - 20%；

稅後 EPS + 6.51 元，低於 2026 年稅後 EPS 預估值 + 8.14 元。

多家海運研調機構 
延續 2026 年 供給過剩看法 
預估 2027 年 持續過剩

基於保守原則 
宏遠假設 
2027 年 BDI 下跌 
裕民 2027 年毛利率 
下滑 6.5 個百分點 
成為預估獲利衰退的主因

若後續 
BDI 沒有重挫 
裕民 2027 年毛利率沒有大減 
則必須明顯上修獲利預估

• AST2700 transition and pricing. AST2700 represents ASPEED’s most 
important product-cycle driver. Management expects shipments to exceed 
10 million units in 2027 and believes AST2700 could account for more than 
half of its major BMC mix in 2028. Indicative ASP is approximately 50% 
above current AST2600. ASPEED implemented an approximately 10% price 
increase from December 1, 2026, following another increase earlier in the 
year. The latest adjustment is intended to offset higher foundry and other 
supplier costs and applies to products shipped after the effective date. 
Because BMCs represent a very small proportion of total server or rack 
costs, management does not expect the increase to materially affect 
customer purchasing decisions. Market-share gains at hyperscale 
customers and continued migration toward AST2700 should allow blended 
ASP to rise even if unit growth moderates. Supply conditions are expected 
to improve around mid-2027, enabling shipments to align more closely 
with underlying customer demand.
• Product mix and GM outlook. Peripheral controllers have expanded faster 
than management initially expected and may now represent 15–20% of 
demand, compared with the previous 10% assumption, per management. 
Growth is coming from networking switches, cooling systems and other 
infrastructure surrounding compute servers. The new AST1840 solution 
combines ASPEED’s management controller with a Lattice FPGA, adding

programmability, hardware security and a smaller footprint for BMC add-in cards. These higher-function products
should carry ASPs above first-generation devices. The GM outlook depends on product mix and cost pass-through
rather than pricing alone. Management indicated that rising input costs will limit the immediate GM benefit of price
increases, but richer AST2700 and peripheral mix should support longer-term profitability.

• Architecture exposure, market turbulence and risks. BMC content varies by system. Management offered rough
planning ratios of approximately one BMC per GPU in certain NVIDIA configurations, one BMC for every three
accelerators in some ASIC platforms, and one BMC per one or two CPUs in conventional servers. More switches,
cooling subsystems and power-management elements could also increase management-controller content even if
the number of compute racks changes. While there is some concern on a solution for IMC  (Infrastructure

Management Controller), ASPEED believes that rack-level control remains fragmented among dedicated servers,
switches, power-rack controllers and software, thus board-level BMC functions cannot be replaced because they
must operate independently when the host processor or operating system fails. Overall, despite rising investor
concerns on customer inventory accumulation and delayed platform deployments, ASPEED's diversification across
GPU, ASIC, CPU and peripheral applications keeps its growth outlook positive.

### Page 3

Aspeed Technology (5274.TWO)
25 November 2026 Citi Research
3
Aspeed Technology
Valuation
We value Aspeed at NT$20,700 based on a historical mid-cycle multiple (since 2020) of 50x average 2027E-28E EPS, as we
believe it fairly reflects the company’s earnings performance and industry dynamics, secular growth trends, and Aspeed’s
strong supply chain position, as well as robust earnings growth amid margin expansions.
Risks
Our quant rating system rates the Aspeed stock High Risk based on its volatility over the past year, which we think is

unwarranted given the company's dominance and outlook in the BMC sector. Key downside risks to our earnings forecasts and
target price include: 1) weaker-than-expected growth in servers; 2) slower-than-expected CPU platform migration; 3) peer
competition; and 4) limited progress in product diversification.
Prepared for Kevin Lu

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.

call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

3597 映興(AVIN)

報告日期：2026/11/17 研 究 員：洪瑞陽 (Ryan Hung) 
壹、訪 談／法 說 內 容 
重點摘要： 
 資料中心新專案自 3Q26 開始放量，3Q26 營收 3.26 億元，QoQ 
+28.8%、YoY +39.6%，稅後 EPS 0.55 元；10 月營收進一步
提高至 1.30 億元，MoM +5.03%、YoY +76.69%，本研究預估
4Q26 營收 3.97 億元，下半年營運動能明顯高於上半年。 
 2027 年新專案進入完整年度貢獻，本研究預估台達電集團訂單
營收占比由 2025 年的 41.5%提升至 2026 年約 50%、2027 年
約 65%，成為未來兩年主要營收增量來源。 
 受惠新訂單放量，本研究預 2026／2027 年營收分別為 11.8 億
元／17.8 億元，2027 年 YOY+50.8%，新增業務以台達電相關
OEM 線束為主，毛利率將維持一定區間較難提升，預估 2026
／2027 年毛利率 23.9%／23.4%，獲利成長主要來自營收放
量，稅後 EPS 分別為 1.61／3.31 元。

一、2H26 新專案開始放量，營收動能明顯轉強 
3Q26 營收 3.26 億元，QoQ +28.8%、YoY +39.6%，毛利率 24.43%，
較 2Q26 維持前期水準；營業利益 2,993 萬元，營業利益率 9.17%，
較 2Q26 的 7.43%提高 1.74 個百分點，稅後淨利 2,156 萬元，EPS 
0.55 元。累計前三季營收 7.78 億元、EPS 0.90 元，營運較 2025
年同期明顯改善。

受惠資料中心新專案持續放量，7／8／9 月營收分別為 9,788 萬元
／1.05 億元／1.24 億元，呈現逐月成長。10 月營收進一步提升至
1.30 億元，MoM +5.03%、YoY +76.69%，創單月新高。本研究依
10 月實際營收、既有業務營運水準及新專案出貨進度推估，4Q26
營收約 3.97 億元，QoQ +21.8%，毛利率 23.65%，營業利益率
11.1%，稅後 EPS 約 0.71 元，下半年營運成長動能持續增強。

二、AI 電力需求成長，新專案放量，2027 年全年貢獻 
AI 算力需求提升帶動資料中心電力密度增加，電源架構朝高功
率、高電流及高可靠度方向發展，HVDC、PDU、UPS／BBU 及
PSU 等電源與備援系統需求同步增加。映興產品已涵蓋 AI 資料中
心機櫃、備援電力模組、基地台機房及電源供應系統等線束模組，
公司產品亦可延伸至大電流與高可靠度電力傳輸應用。

映興在 AI 資料中心的供應模式以電源系統相關線束及連接元件為

主，並非直接供應 CSP，因此營運成長主要隨下游客戶資料中心
電源系統出貨放大。本研究預期 2026 年新專案僅貢獻部分年度，
2027 年除具全年化效果外，出貨仍持續 Ramp，因此預估新專案
相關營收較 2026 年成長逾 2 倍，帶動台達電集團訂單營收占比由
2025 年的約四成提高至 2026 年約 50%、2027 年約 65%，成為未
來兩年主要營收增量來源。

三、既有應用維持成長，較高毛利產品支撐產品組合 
除 AI 資料中心外，公司既有產品橫跨工業應用、移動載具、車載
電子及儲能等領域。移動載具涵蓋無人機、機器狗、自行車、摩
托車、滑板車及船外機等所需的防水連接器與線束模組；車載電
子則供應車用風扇、OBC 及煞車系統線束；工業應用包括氫燃料
電池、馬達、機械手臂與家電相關線束及代理元件。

公司自有防水連接器累積 93 項國內外專利，具微型大電流、高插
拔次數、IP68 防水及抗火花等特性；OEM 線束則可透過自主運算
工具進行阻抗與結構優化設計，並取得 ISO 9001、ISO 14001、IATF 
16949 及 UL 等認證。

本研究預期車載及移動載具等較高毛利產品持續成長，可部分抵
銷 AI 資料中心新增 OEM 線束占比提高造成的毛利率負向影響。
3Q26 實際毛利率仍達 24.43%，顯示新增訂單放量尚未造成毛利率
明顯下降；惟隨 2027 年 OEM 營收占比進一步提高，產品組合仍
有毛利率些許負向影響。因此映興未來兩年營運型態將呈現「營
收快速成長、毛利率大致維持、費用率下降」，獲利成長主要來
自營收規模放大，而非毛利率擴張。

四、預估 2026／2027 年稅後 EPS 1.61／3.31 元 
2026 年受惠資料中心新專案自 2H26 開始貢獻，本研究預估全年
營收 11.75 億元，YoY +36.5%；毛利率 23.9%、營業費用約 1.82
億元，費用率由 2025 年的 17.4%降至 15.5%，帶動營業利益約 9,837
萬元、營業利益率提升至 8.37%，EPS 1.61 元。

2027 年新專案進入完整年度貢獻，本研究預估全年營收 17.75 億
元，YoY +51.1%；毛利率 23.4%、營業費用約 2.20 億元，費用率
進一步降至 12.4%，營業利益約 1.95 億元、營業利益率 11.0%。
預估稅後淨利約 1.31 億元，EPS 3.31 元，YoY 約+106%。 
五、投資建議：買進，目標價 59 元 
隨台達電訂單新專案貢獻，映興有新的營運動能，帶動營收與 EPS
正向成長，以 2027 年 EPS3.31 計算，根據歷史區間與同業評價，
給予 18 倍本益比，目標價 59 元

貳、產 業 及 營 運 概 況 
一、公司簡介 
映興電子成立於 1986 年，主要從事連接線束模組、代理元件及自
有防水連接器，營運據點涵蓋台灣、中國深圳與昆山、泰國及美
國。公司產品可分為三類：第一為 OEM／ODM／OBM 連接線束
模組；第二為代理元件，包括 Sensata 溫度保護元件、日本 Kurabe
特殊電線及 Anderson Power 大電流連接器；第三為自有防水連接
器。公司目前聚焦 AI 伺服器、工業應用、移動載具、車載電子及
綠能儲能五大應用市場。

公司核心能力在於複雜線束加工、防水連接器設計及代理元件整
合。自有防水連接器累積 93 項國內外專利，具備 IP68 防水、抗
火花、高插拔次數及微型大電流等特性；OEM 線束則可透過自主
運算工具進行阻抗與結構優化設計，並取得 ISO 9001、ISO 14001、
IATF 16949 及 UL 等認證。相較一般單純線材加工廠，公司可利
用一線品牌代理權作為客戶導入入口，再延伸至線束加工與自有
連接器，提高單一客戶合作深度。

五大應用中，AI 伺服器主要供應資料中心 HVDC／PDU／CDU 電
源機櫃、UPS／BBU 備援電池櫃、TPS 基地台機房及 PSU 內部線
束；工業應用涵蓋氫燃料電池、馬達、機械手臂及家電所需線束
與代理元件；移動載具包括無人機、機器狗、自行車、摩托車、
滑板車及船外機所需防水連接器與線束；車載電子則供應車用風
扇、OBC 及煞車系統線束；綠能儲能則以太陽能儲能、大電流連
接器及電池櫃線束為主，相關技術亦可延伸至 AI 資料中心 BBU
備援電力應用。

資料來源：公司官網

二、AI 新專案放量，台達電集團訂單營收占比持續提升 
AI 資料中心為映興目前最重要的新增成長來源。隨 AI 算力需求
提升，資料中心電力系統逐步朝高功率、高電流與高可靠度方向
發展，HVDC、PDU、UPS／BBU 及 PSU 等電源與備援系統需求
同步增加。公司目前相關產品已涵蓋 AI 資料中心機櫃、備援電力
模組、基地台機房及電源供應系統等線束模組，因此可隨下游資
料中心電源系統需求擴張而受惠。公司亦已具備 UPS 電池系統、
IT Rack、PDU 及電池模組串接等產品經驗，可延伸至 AI 資料中
心大電流及高可靠度電力傳輸應用。

公司並非直接供應 CSP，而是透過台達電集團訂單切入終端雲端
服務業者供應鏈。映興供應內容集中於資料中心電源系統內部線
束與連接元件，因此營運節奏與台達電集團資料中心電源業務高
度連動。除了資料中心電源機櫃外，公司在台達電集團既有業務
亦涵蓋 PSU、車載充電系統、基地台機房、儲能櫃及自動化設備
等產品，客戶合作範圍並非僅限於 AI 資料中心單一應用。

本研究估算，2025 年台達電集團訂單營收占比約四成；2026 年上
半年新專案尚未正式進入主要營收貢獻期，因此相關營收仍以
TPS、車用及既有電源產品為主。隨資料中心新專案於 3Q26 開始
放量，7、8、9 月公司合併營收逐月提升，3Q26 營收達 3.26 億元，
QoQ +28.8%。10 月營收進一步增加至 1.30 億元，MoM +5.03%、
YoY +76.69%，本研究預期台達電集團訂單相關營收成長動能自下
半年起明顯轉強。

本研究對資料中心新專案採 Bottom-up 推估，將台達電集團訂單
營收區分為既有業務與資料中心新增業務，並依已公告營收、既
有業務正常營運水準及後續專案 Ramp 節奏進行推估，而非直接
以客戶占比反推公司營收。由於 2026 年新專案僅貢獻部分年度，

5 
2027 年同時享有全年化與出貨增加兩項效果，本研究預估相關新
專案營收較 2026 年成長逾 2 倍，帶動台達電集團訂單營收占比由
2026 年約 50%進一步提高至 2027 年約 65%，為未來兩年最主要
營收增量來源。

惟台達電集團訂單相關業務主要仍以 OEM 線束為主，毛利率相對
低於自有防水連接器、車載及部分移動載具產品，因此台達電集
團訂單營收占比快速提升將對產品組合形成一定毛利壓力。不
過，3Q26 毛利率仍達 24.43%，維持前期水準，加上集團既有產能
可因應新增訂單，營收規模放大有助於固定製造成本攤提。本研
究預估 2026／2027 年毛利率約 23.9%／23.4%，營業利益率則由
8.3%提高至 11.0%，顯示本輪成長主要來自 AI 帶動營收放量及費
用率下降，而非毛利率成長。 
三、車載與移動載具高毛利產品及其他業務正向發展 
移動載具產品涵蓋無人機、機器狗、自行車、摩托車、滑板車、
輪椅及船外機所需的防水連接器與線束模組。公司在此領域主要
競爭力來自自有防水連接器，可提供 IP68、防水、抗火花及高插
拔次數設計，適合戶外、震動及高濕度等環境，產品附加價值高
於一般 OEM 線束。公司原有兩輪載具為既有核心市場，近年亦逐
步向無人機與四足機器人等新應用延伸。

考量自行車庫存逐步正常化、無人機非紅供應鏈需求提高，以及
機器狗等新型移動載具應用擴張，本研究預估移動載具中短期營
收可維持中個位數成長，並以約 5～8%作為 2027 年相關業務成長
假設。由於此類產品較多採用公司自有防水連接器及客製化線
束，其毛利結構優於一般 OEM 產品，因此除營收成長外，亦可作
為未來產品組合的重要支撐。

車載電子方面，公司主要供應車用風扇、On-board Charger 及煞車
系統線束，已取得 IATF 16949 車用認證，可切入對品質、可靠度
及認證要求較高的汽車供應鏈。公司現有產品已涵蓋車載充電、
煞車系統及相關電力電子應用，隨全球汽車朝電動化與智慧化發
展，電力電子、充電系統及熱管理需求仍具中長期成長趨勢。

考量車用產品認證週期較長、導入後客戶黏著度相對較高，加上
電動車及高階車用電子滲透率持續提升，本研究預估車載電子中
長期營收成長約 10～15%。部分高階車用產品專案能見度亦延伸
至未來數年，預期車載業務可持續成為公司較穩定、且毛利率優
於一般 OEM 線束的產品線。

工業應用為公司既有營收的重要來源，產品涵蓋氫燃料電池、馬

或個股之相關條件改變，投資人需自行考量投資之實際狀況與風險，本公司恕不負擔任何法律責任及做任何保

達、機械手臂、燃油引擎所需線束模組與代理元件，以及空調壓
縮機、吹風機、吸塵器等家電使用的代理元件；客戶應用橫跨燃
料電池、工業自動化與消費型家電。

本研究預期工業應用仍受惠工業自動化、燃料電池及資料中心相
關電力需求，維持穩健成長。不過須注意公司應用別與客戶別分
類並非完全對應，部分 AI 資料中心及基礎建設相關產品亦可能歸
類於工業應用，因此本研究在財測時不直接套用單一工業應用成
長率，以避免與台達電集團訂單 AI 專案重複計算。

綠能儲能方面，公司產品包括太陽能儲能系統、大電流儲能連接
器及電池櫃串接線束，其技術與 AI 資料中心 BBU 在大電流傳輸、
電池模組串接及高可靠度連接等方面具有共通性。隨儲能與資料
中心備援電力需求增加，公司過去累積的電池櫃及大電流連接技
術可同時支援傳統儲能及 AI 資料中心相關應用，提供中長期新增
業務機會。

整體而言，本研究預期其他客戶業務將由車載、移動載具、工業
及儲能等既有應用共同支撐。2026 年在既有應用回升下，預估其
他客戶營收約 5.86 億元，YoY 約+16.5%；考量 2027 年基期提高，
且工業分類中部分 AI 營收已另於台達電集團訂單模型估算，本研
究採較保守成長假設，預估 2027 年其他客戶營收約 6.27 億元，
YoY 約+7%。此部分雖非未來兩年最大營收增量來源，但較高毛
利產品比重有助於抵銷 AI OEM 線束占比上升造成的產品組合壓
力。 
叁、業 績 及 獲 利 預 估 
一、預估 2026 年稅後 EPS 1.61 元 
本研究將營收拆分為「台達電集團訂單」與「其他客戶」兩部分。
台達電集團訂單再區分既有業務及資料中心新專案；其他客戶則
依車載、移動載具、工業及儲能等既有應用的實際營運水準與成
長趨勢估算。台達電集團訂單營收占比為兩部分個別預估後除算
得出，而非預先假設占比後回推營收。

3Q26 營收 3.26 億元，QoQ +28.8%、YoY +39.6%，較本研究原預
估 3.22 億元高約 1.5%；毛利率 24.43%，優於原預估 23.2%，惟營
業費用 4,976 萬元，亦高於原預估 4,200 萬元，營業利益 2,993 萬
元、營業利益率 9.17%，稅後 EPS 0.55 元。雖然營收與毛利率優
於原預估，但費用同步增加，顯示新增訂單放量後仍需考量營運
規模擴大所帶來的費用成長。

10 月營收 1.30 億元，MoM +5.03%、YoY +76.69%，亦高於本研究

原估約 1.23 億元。考量資料中心新專案出貨進度優於原先預期，
本研究上修 4Q26 營收至 3.97 億元，QoQ +21.8%。毛利率考量 3Q26
實際維持 24%以上，且產能利用率提高有助於固定製造成本攤
提，預估 4Q26 毛利率 24.0%；營業費用則依 3Q26 實際水準估約
5,000 萬元，營業利益約 4,534 萬元、營業利益率 11.4%，稅 後 EPS
約 0.73 元。下半年營收合計約 7.24 億元，較上半年增加約 60.3%。

本研究預估 2026 全年營收 11.75 億元，YoY +36.5%，其中台達電
集團訂單營收約 5.89 億元，占營收比重約 50%；其他客戶營收約
5.86 億元，YoY 約+16.5%，維持雙位數成長。

雖新增台達電集團訂單以 OEM 線束為主，產品組合對毛利率形成
一定的負向影響，但 3Q26 實際毛利率仍維持 24.43%，反映營收
規模增加可部分抵銷產品組合影響。本研究預估 2026 年毛利率約
23.9%，較 2025 年的 23.6%小幅提升。營業費用預估約 1.82 億元，
YoY 約+21.9%，增幅低於營收，費用率由 2025 年的 17.4%下降至
15.5%，帶動營業利益約 1.00 億元、營業利益率提升至 8.37%。

業外損益預估約-598 萬元，考量前三季實際稅率及 4Q26 有效稅率
35%的假設，預估全年有效稅率約 31.7%，稅後淨利約 6,404 萬元，
EPS 1.63 元。獲利成長主要來自營收放量、固定成本攤提改善及
營業費用率下降，而非單純依賴毛利率提升。

二、預估 2027 年稅後 EPS 3.31 元  
2027 年營收成長主要來自資料中心新專案。由於 2026 年新專案僅
自下半年開始貢獻，2027 年除完整貢獻全年外，出貨仍持續 Ramp 
up。本研究考量 3Q26 實際營收與 10 月營收均優，預估新專案相
關營收較 2026 年成長逾 2 倍；既有台達電集團訂單業務則維持穩
定，預估全年台達電集團訂單營收約 11.48 億元。

其他客戶方面，本研究預估 2026 年營收約 5.86 億元，2027 年考
量車載、移動載具及其他既有業務持續成長，但 2026 已有較高基
期，因此採較正常化成長假設，預估全年營收約 6.27 億元，YoY
約+7%。

兩者加總後，預估 2027 年營收 17.75 億元，YoY +51.1%，台達電
集團訂單營收占比提高至約 65%。相較原預估 16.94 億元，本次主
要反映資料中心新專案出貨進度提前，而非 ASP 上升。

季度營收預估呈逐季上升，1Q27／2Q27／3Q27／4Q27 分別為 4.05
／4.29／4.58／4.83 億元。新專案出貨增加使台達電集團訂單營收
占比逐步提高，但考量 OEM 比重上升，毛利率維持；另一方面，

新增營收有助於產能利用率及固定成本攤提改善，本研究預估四
季毛利率分別為 23.55%／ 23.43%／ 23.35%／ 23.25%，全年約
23.39%。

營業費用方面，本研究依 3Q26 實際費用及 4Q26 預估費用水準進
行推估，考量營運規模擴大，預估 1Q27／2Q27／3Q27／4Q27 營
業費用分別為 5,200／5,400／5,600／5,820 萬元，全年約 2.20 億
元，YoY 約+20.8%；惟營收增幅高於費用，預估全年費用率由 2026
年的 15.5%降至 12.4%，帶動營業利益約 1.95 元、營業利益率
11.0%。

業外收入預估約 500 萬元，有效稅率維持 35%，預估 2027 年稅後
淨利約 1.31 億元，EPS 3.33 元，YoY 約+104%。季度 EPS 預估分
別為 0.74／0.80／0.87／0.93 元，營業利益率隨營收放量由 1Q27
的 10.7%逐步提高至 4Q27 的 11.3%，顯示本輪獲利成長仍主要由
營業槓桿帶動。 
三、台達電集團訂單為主要成長，其他業務維持穩健  
台達電集團訂單方面，本研究預估既有業務維持正常營運水準，
資料中心新專案則自 2H26 進入放量階段。考量 3Q26 實際營收及
10 月營收均優，預估 2026／2027 年台達電集團訂單營收分別為
5.89／11.48 億元，YoY 約+65%／+95%，占合併營收比重由 2026
年的約 50%提高至 2027 年約 65%，為未來兩年最主要營收增量來
源。

其他客戶方面，本研究依公司既有應用營運趨勢進行估算，預估
2026 年營收約 5.86 億元，2027 年增加至約 6.27 億元，YoY 約+7%。
2027 年增速較 2026 正常化，主要考量 2026 已有較高基期。

周建霖 
(886-2)2563-1666 
mark.chou@firstsec.com.tw

貿聯-KY(3665) 
買進, TP 2,900 (潛在報酬 90.79%)  
買進 
Price(11/13):1,520 
Target Price：2900

高毛利產品合計來到 59%，此外營運槓桿帶來的效益使本業獲利
率創歷史新高。Interplex 已完整併表，單月貢獻金額來到 6700
萬美金，除了擴大產品線外，合併後同樣有助於加深既有客戶的合
作關係。展望 2027 年，HPC 事業將迎來產品價量齊揚，尤其以高
毛利的 Power 產品最為明顯，根據客戶規劃，1.6T AEC 也將逐步
起量，因此可預期 HPC 占比將會再進一步提升，利於整體營運表
現。綜合以上，研究部預估貿聯 2026 年 EPS 76.23 元，2027 年
EPS 131.41 元，目前 PE 為 11.6x，參考歷史 PE 區間為 10-35x，
考量貿聯獲利將維持高成長期，唯獨近期市場擔憂資料中心建置速
度恐受高利率環境而放緩，存在一定 AI 伺服器延宕部屬之風險，
因此研究部下修 PER 至 22x，目標價 2900 元，維持買進。 
⚫ 公司基本資料 ⚫ 公司基本資料 
貿聯-KY(3665)成立於 1996 年，為全球連接線大廠，總部位於美
國矽谷，生產據點則廣泛分布在全球 37 地。產品應用包含數據通
訊、工廠自動化、半導體設備、醫療、車用等，2025 年工業佔 35%、
通訊 42%、車用 11%、電器 11%、其他 1%。銷售主要為美國 20%、
中國 20%、德國 16%、馬來西亞 7%、台灣 3%、其他 34%。

此外 AEC 也正式恢復量產加上 800G 滲透率的提高，同樣帶動

殖利率(%) 0.87 HPC 部門成長 Q+30%，Y+61%，SPE 也延續上半年以來的穩健
成長幅度，Q+11%，Y+70%，兩高利潤事業合計貢獻營收占比進
一步攀升至 59%，Q+3ppts，Y+8ppts，在營收成長帶來的營運槓
桿效益下也反映在營益率創公司歷史新高。

Interplex 正式併表，產品組合有望再度優化 
貿聯 10 月營收 118.5 億元，M+26.9%，Y+80.0%，自 9 月起完成
對 Interplex 的收購，10 月已完整貢獻營收，單月貢獻金額高達
6700 萬美金，約 20 億台幣，由於 Interplex 既有客戶與貿聯本身
有所重疊，然而產品項目卻有所不同，預計在透過集團整合能力下
將進一步推動公司在客戶群內滲透率的提升。此外貿聯本身新規格
的 Power 產品也持續提高出貨，研究部預期產品組合有望再度優
化，進一步推升獲利率表現。綜合以上，研究部預估貿聯 26Q4 營
收 349.1 億元，Q+25.3%，Y+76.5%，毛利率 33.84%，稅後淨利
56.7 億元，Q+32.0%，Y+106.8%，EPS 28.39 元；2026 年營收
1069.3 億元，Y+50.1%，毛利率 31.83%，稅後淨利 152.1 億元，
Y+69.0%，EPS 76.23 元。

2027 年 HPC 產品維持價量齊揚，光通訊產品 2028 年有望發酵 
隨著 Vera Rubin 進入放量，Rack Busbar 升級至 5000A，Power 
Whip 規格從 60A 升級至 100A 且用量增加，1.6T AEC 也將逐步
開始貢獻營收，另外 Interplex 的加入所帶來的產品優化和客戶關
係的綁定，研究部認為貿聯 HPC 事業仍將迎來顯著的價量齊揚增
長。光傳輸領域上貿聯也持續耕耘，除了新富生 MPO 相關產品，
OCP 上與 ams Osram 的合作也意味著公司已著手布局下一代
Scale up 的通訊方案，研究部看好 wide-and-slow 架構在未來的導
入機會以及市場，預計 2028 年後的發酵有望帶來營運成長動能。
綜合以上，研究部預估貿聯 2027 年營收 1608.0 億元，Y+50.4%，

結論 
高毛利產品合計來到 59%，此外營運槓桿帶來的效益使本業獲利率創歷
史新高。Interplex 已完整併表，單月貢獻金額來到 6700 萬美金，除了
擴大產品線外，合併後同樣有助於加深既有客戶的合作關係。展望 2027
年，HPC 事業將迎來產品價量齊揚，尤其以高毛利的 Power 產品最為明
顯，根據客戶規劃，1.6T AEC 也將逐步起量，因此可預期 HPC 占比將
會再進一步提升，利於整體營運表現。綜合以上，研究部預估貿聯 2026
年 EPS 76.23 元，2027 年 EPS 131.41 元，目前 PE 為 11.6x，參考歷
史 PE 區間為 10-35x，考量貿聯獲利將維持高成長期，唯獨近期市場擔
憂資料中心建置速度恐受高利率環境而放緩，存在一定 AI 伺服器延宕部
屬之風險，因此研究部下修 PER 至 22x，目標價 2900 元，維持買進。

## Document 202: [000270] 기아(000270) 3Q26Re_ 고난의 행군 너머를 볼 수 있는가기아(000270) 3Q26Re_ 고난의 행군 너머를 볼 수 있는가3Q26Re_ 고난의 행군 너머를_64be78d2

### Page 1

금융투자분석사의 확인 및 중요 공시는 Appendix 참조

 절대 (달러환산) (5.2) (2.7) 0.2

3Q26 영업이익 1.8조, 핵심 하락 원인은 인센티브 
환율효과는 전분기 대비 -5,330억 작용했으나, 기말환율 하락에 따른 판매보증비의 급감 
(6,200억, -9,250억 qoq)으로 상쇄되었다 판단. 인센티브 비용 증가세는 -6,060억원의 영
향을 미쳤으며, 대부분 유럽지역 EV2 판매를 위한 딜러 인센티브에서 발생 추정. 
외생변수는 능력 밖의 일, EV 전략에 대해서는 장기성장에 무게 
중동 분쟁 지속, 환율 하락과 같은 외생변수의 부정적 영향을 제외한다면 단기 영업적 측면
의 실적 악재는 BEV 인센티브 지급기조의 변화가 없을 것이라는 점. 중장기 글로벌 BEV 
시장의 입지 강화를 단기 전사 수익성보다 중시하는 전략을 채택. 
마찬가지로, 4분기가 환율 효과의 본격화 
3Q26과 반대로 qoq 기말환율 대비 낙폭 확대할 평균환율 (QTD 1,338원) 효과로 환관련 
감익 본격화 예상. 즉, 판매보증비의 정상화 (qoq 반등)로 인해 수익성 추가 악화 가능성 존
재. 완성차 판매량의 경우 경쟁사 대비 견조했던 2Q-3Q26의 기조를 유지할 것이라 예상. 
투자의견 BUY 유지, 목표주가 129,000원으로 하향 
목표주가 하향 근거는 산정 기준을 FY27F로 변경함과 동시에 FY27F 자동차사업 NOPAT 
예상치 하향과 현대모비스 지분가치 하락. 밸류에이션상 저점에 도달했다는 의견은 FY27F 
EPS의 yoy 하락으로 설득력 약화. 중장기 BEV 시장 입지 강화에 대한 밸류에이션 프리미
엄 반영은 글로벌 전동화 속도에 달려 있음. 주가 상승 트리거는 1) 외생변수의 개선, 2) 신
차 출시를 통한 상품성 강화, 3) BEV 시장 점유율 확대 이후 점진적 인센티브 축소, 4) 20조
원 수준의 순현금 기반 발생 가능한 사업적 결정.

EV/EBITDA 11.1  13.8  15.8  14.3  
배당수익률 4.1  3.4  3.4  4.1  
ROE 12.4  8.4  6.8  7.7

주가 추이

3Q26 매출액과 영업이익은 비현금성 비용 항목 개선으로 시장 기대치
를 상회했으나, 차량 판매 감소와 인플레이션 등으로 연간 가이던스는 
하향되었습니다. 올해 생산 차질에 따라 이연된 신차 판매를 ’27년에 
확대하며 매출/이익 회복을 가시화하는 부분에 주목 필요합니다. 
매출 성장은 금융부문이, 영업이익은 자동차부문이 기여 
동사 3Q26 매출액은 45.1조원(-3.5% 이하 YoY), 영업이익 2.68조원
(+5.5%)을 기록하며 시장 기대치를 상회했다. 파업으로 인한 생산 차질
로 3Q 판매량 92.1만대(-11.3%)에 그치며 자동차부문 매출은 큰 폭으
로 감소(33조원, -10.0%)했으나, 금융부문 매출이 취급 자산 증가로 큰 
폭으로 성장(+27.8%)했다. 반면, 영업이익은 금리 상승에 따른 금융부문 
영업비용 확대 영향과 기타부문 실적 부진 발생과 달리 자동차부문은 전
년 동기 대비 관세율 차이에 따른 비용 감소와 판매보증 충당부채 전입
액 감소 등 우호적 환율효과로 큰 폭의 증가세(+24.8%) 기록했다. 
’26년 가이던스 하향, 구조적 반등 요인은 아직 
3Q 실적 발표에서 주목할 부분은 가이던스 하향과 현금흐름이다. 연간 
판매목표를 연초 415.8만대에서 400만대로 하향했고 영업이익률 목표
는 6.3~7.3%에서 5.5~6.5%로 0.8%p 하향했으며, ’26년 투자계획은 
17.8조원에서 16조원으로 낮췄다. 특히, 투자계획 하향은 차량 판매 매
출 감소 대비 인센티브, 원자재 가격 상승 등으로 현금흐름이 악화된 
부분에 기인한 것으로 판단된다. 실적발표에서 전사적 원가 최적화 활
동 추진을 강조했으나, 이는 좀 더 장기적 측면에서 효과가 가시화될 
것으로 판단되며, 인플레이션 및 원화절상 상황 고려 시 구조적 반등은 
단기적으로 쉽지 않을 수 있다. 
절치부심의 2027년, 목표주가 600.000원 유지 
그러나 매크로 상황을 제외하면  ’26년 하반기 생산 차질에 따른 신차 
판매효과가 ’27년으로 이연된 것이라는 점에 주목 필요하다.   ‘북미 HEV, 유럽 
BEV’ 전략 하에서 엘란트라, 투싼 풀세인지, GV80, G80 등 고수익 HEV 차종 
중심 미국 수요 대응을 통해 Mix 개선하는 모습이 확인되고, 생산이 본격화된 
아이오닉3 유럽 판매 확대 시 중국 로컬업체들의 공세를 방어하는 모습을 
확인하는 것이 가능할 것으로 판단한다. 노후차종 중심 운영에 따른 판매 둔화 
이외에 아직 근원적 판매 경쟁력 저하로는 판단하지 않는다.

반영했습니다. 본인은 이 자료에서 다룬 종목과 관련해 공표일 현재 관련 법규상 알려야 할 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 본인은 이 
자료를 기관투자자 또는 제 3 자에게 사전에 제공한 사실이 없습니다. (김성래) 
저희 회사는 공표일 현재 이 자료에서 다룬 종목의 발행주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
이 자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위해 당사 고객에 한하여 배포되는 자료로서 저작권이 당사에 있으며 불법 복제 및 배포를 금
합니다. 이 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료나 정보출처로부터 얻은 것이지만, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완
전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 이 자료는 어떠한 경우에도 고객의 증권투자 결과와 관련된 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙으로 사
용될 수 없습니다.

[ 종목 투자등급 ] 
당사는 개별 종목에 대해 향후 1 년간 +15% 이상의 절대수익률이 기대되는 종목에 대해 Buy(매수) 의견을 제시합니다. 또한 절대수
익률 -15~+15%가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Hold(보유) 의견을, -15% 이하가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Sell(매도) 의견을 제시합니다. 밸류
에이션 방법 등 절대수익률 산정은 개별 종목을 커버하는 애널리스트의 추정에 따르며, 목표주가 산정이나 투자의견 변경 주기는 종
목별로 다릅니다.

[ 산업 투자의견 ] 
당사는 산업에 대해 향후 1 년간 해당 업종의 수익률이 과거 수익률에 비해 양호한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Positive(긍정
적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 또한 향후 1 년간 수익률이 과거 수익률과 유사한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Neutral(중립적) 
의견을, 과거 수익률보다 부진한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Negative(부정적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 산업별 수익률 전
망은 해당 산업 내 분석대상 종목들에 대한 담당 애널리스트의 분석과 판단에 따릅니다.

## Document 254: A Thanksgiving Serving Of Economic Optimism

Source PDF: all report pdf/A Thanksgiving Serving Of Economic Optimism.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-11-26
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: A Thanksgiving Serving Of Economic Optimism
Date:    8 hours ago
A Thanksgiving Serving Of Economic Optimism

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
A Thanksgiving Serving Of
Economic Optimism
By Ed Yardeni & Elias Griepentrog • 25 Nov 2026
View in browser
Photo by Jed Owen / Unsplash

Thanksgiving is still scheduled for tomorrow, but the Macy's
Thanksgiving Day Parade has been postponed from Thursday to
Saturday because a severe coastal storm is expected to bring
heavy rain and gusty winds to New York. We wish you a safe and

### Page 2

happy Thanksgiving with your family and friends. We are very
thankful for your interest in our research.
I. Investors Should Be Thankful
Ahead of Thanksgiving, investors should have been thankful that
corporate earnings are so strong. They should be feasting on industry
analysts' expectations that earnings will remain strong over the next two
years. Instead, investors have lost their appetite for such optimism. That's
plain to see in the plunging forward P/Es of the major stock market

indexes in recent weeks (chart). They are clearly worried that analysts'
consensus earnings expectations are irrationally exuberant. With valuation
multiples now as low as during the bear market of 2020 and the significant
correction of 2018, they seem to be anticipating that the AI buildout, which
has been boosting the economy, will now push it into a recession.
That's a plausible concern. In the past, falling valuation multiples
(determined by investors) have often anticipated recessions, while

consensus earnings estimates (determined by analysts) have lagged
behind. However, investors were wrong in 2018 and in 2020. Both times,
the widely anticipated recession didn't materialize. We expect a similar
outcome this time, and that the current correction won't turn into a bear
market. So it should be the post-midterms buying opportunity we first
anticipated on September 15.

### Page 3

A relatively short correction scenario is supported by how quickly
pessimistic sentiment has hit the tape. The Investors Intelligence Bull/Bear
Ratio recently peaked during the week of August 17 at 3.88 (chart). It
plunged to 1.13 this past week. From a contrarian perspective, that's a
very bullish reading. However, it has fallen below 1.00 and stayed there
for a few weeks during bear markets and long corrections.
Stock investors aren't the only ones fretting about an AI-led recession. The

2-year and 10-year US Treasury yields have dropped sharply since early
last week by 19bps and 28bps (chart). A 25bps Fed rate hike is still
expected in early December, but the odds of a 50bps hike are down to
zero, according to the CME Group's FedWatch tool. We've been saying
that the Fed should do 50bps, but we concede that may be far-fetched
now.

### Page 4

Also signaling slower economic growth ahead is the recent decline in the
S&P Goldman Sachs nonprecious metals spot price index (chart).
II. Cornucopia Of Booming Economic Indicators
While investors are increasingly bracing for a significant cyclical slowdown
or downturn in 2027, the latest indicators show that the economy
continues to fire on all cylinders. The economy has passed numerous
stress tests since the start of the Roaring 2020s. We think it will pass the

current ones, including the latest AI Panic Attack. We see the AI buildout
slowing, not stopping. Consumers are likely to continue to do what they do
best. The federal government deficit remains very stimulative. Here's
more:
(1) GDP. Q3 real GDP growth was revised up sharply from 3.4% to 4.3%
(saar), the strongest quarterly growth rate since Q3-2023 (chart).

### Page 5

Real consumer spending growth was revised up from 3.8% to 4.2%
(saar), the fastest pace since Q4-2024 (chart). Real nonresidential fixed
investment growth was revised up from 12.7% to 16.1%. As a result, real
final sales to private domestic purchasers rose a blockbuster 5.8%. Keep
in mind that the net impact on GDP of some of the surge in capital
spending on semiconductors was offset by a surge in semiconductor
imports.
(2) Corporate profits. After-tax corporate profits from current production

rose to a record $4.0 trillion (saar) in Q3, with undistributed profits
reaching a record $1.8 trillion (chart). Corporate America shows few signs
of an impending cyclical downturn.

### Page 6

(3) Consumer spending and income. Real consumer spending was
essentially unchanged in October, remaining near its record high (chart).
Real disposable personal income (DPI) rose a solid 0.5% m/m, the
strongest monthly increase since January. The ongoing retirement of the
Baby Boomers should increasingly weigh on real DPI growth.
Real goods spending fell 0.2% m/m in October, led by energy goods
(-1.2%) and furnishings (-0.6%), while services spending edged up 0.1%.

Consumers continued to spend on fun, with recreational goods and
vehicles up 1.1% and recreation services up 0.4% (chart).

### Page 7

The personal saving rate rebounded to 4.1% in October as income growth
outpaced spending growth (chart). Nevertheless, we expect it to continue
trending lower as more Baby Boomers retire. They no longer earn labor
income but continue to spend their sizeable accumulated wealth.
(4) Labor market. The labor market is in good shape. Initial
unemployment claims fell to 189,000 last week, matching July's reading,
the lowest since September 1969. Continuing claims fell to a subdued 1.7

million (chart).

### Page 8

III. Inflation Remains Stuck
The downside of the economy's remarkable strength is that inflation
remains stubbornly above 3.0%. Headline PCED inflation rose 0.3% m/m
and 3.8% y/y in October, well above the Fed's 2% inflation target (chart).
Core PCED inflation rose 0.3% m/m and 3.3% y/y, with the latter the
highest since November 2023.
Goods PCED inflation rose to 4.4% y/y in October, matching May's
reading, the highest since January 2023 (chart). We expect the AI

buildout, elevated energy prices, deglobalization, rising defense spending
amid geopolitical tensions, and tariffs to keep goods inflation elevated.
Today's proposed Section 301 tariffs on 14 major US trading partners,

### Page 9

including China, Japan, and the EU, confirm that the tariff story is far from
over.
Services PCED inflation rose to 3.6% y/y in October, with housing and
utilities inflation rebounding to 3.3%. Supercore services inflation, which
excludes energy and housing, has been trending higher since October
2025, reaching 3.6% last month (chart).
We expect strong aggregate demand, an improving labor market, and the
ongoing pass-through of higher energy costs to keep services inflation

elevated before productivity growth provides a more meaningful
disinflationary tailwind. The recent upturn in market rent inflation suggests
that the disinflationary tailwind from shelter is turning into a headwind
(chart).

### Page 10

clause.
Yardeni QuickTakes © 2026 – Unsubscribe

### Page 2

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC INSIGHT
• Unemployment and output both show a common pattern
of downturns being short, sharp and irregular, while the
subsequent recoveries and expansions are longer and
slower. The eminent economist Milton Friedman likened
this pattern to plucking on a string. The causes are unclear,

though there are some plausible candidates.
• If we take the “plucking” pattern seriously, there are clear
policy implications. Standard models used by central banks
do not allow for this pattern and therefore systematically
overstate how often capacity is tight. And when a
downturn does occur, the “plucking” view argues for
forceful stabilisation policy.
• We believe the current slow upward grind in
unemployment in Australia is a different phenomenon

and expect slow growth rather than a sharp downturn. If
people start thinking current inflation pressures demand a
1980s-style “Volcker disinflation”, though, it could just end
up plucking the economy.
Look at a graph of the unemployment rate for almost any
western economy, and you will notice a few things. The
big increases are sharp and sudden, while the subsequent
recoveries are slow and last longer. The timing of those upward
spikes is irregular, with no obvious pattern. The height of the

spike in unemployment is a good predictor of how far the rate
declines in the recovery that follows. By contrast, neither the
length of an expansion phase of declining unemployment, nor
the size of the decline predict the size of the next downturn.
This pattern is a far cry from the notion of a business
cycle, where output and unemployment oscillate round
some average or trend. There is something different about
downturns. They are not just like expansions but a bit slower.

The eminent economist Milton Friedman described this as a
“plucking” model. The economy, he suggested, was a bit like a
guitar string. Most of the time it stayed close to the fretboard.
There is a physical maximum amount of production possible
at any one time. Occasionally, though, the economy could be
plucked away from that maximum, with output falling and
unemployment rising sharply.
Remarkably, the mainstream macroeconomic literature that
central banks rely on has engaged little with this feature of the

data. Forecasting models used by the RBA and its peers do not
produce this pattern. Some authors have looked at “plucking”-
type models, though. Their analysis confirmed that downturns
predict the size of the following expansion, but the reverse
Is the economy ‘plucked’?
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group
is not true. Expansions do not “die of old age” by building up
imbalances. Australia went for 30 years without a proper
downturn: it turns out that this is entirely possible provided

nothing bad enough happens.
These authors further argue that the maximum production level
during expansions is buffeted by small shocks, some temporary,
others positive and permanent – think new technologies and
ways of doing things, or a policy change that encourages
workforce participation. Downturns, by contrast, are triggered
by large shocks that are usually temporary and arrive at irregular
intervals. Some argue that it is best to think of economies as

having three phases: normal expansions, which can continue
for long periods; shorter-lived downturns; and subsequent
recovery phases where growth can outstrip normal rates and
unemployment declines, but at a slower pace than it rose.
Some downturns do not end in full recovery, however. This
observation links to another strand of research showing that
the output losses associated with financial crises and wars are
not fully recovered. The experience after the Global Financial

Crisis shows this pattern in a number of advanced economies.
Standard models would interpret this as slower growth in
potential output, perhaps feeding the Australian tradition of
hand-wringing about insufficient productivity or reform.
While the pattern in the data is clear, the causes of “plucking”
are far from settled, with some authors seeing boom–bust
dynamics as inherent in the economic system rather than
driven by shocks. Some recent papers – including preliminary

RBA work – look to certain frictions in the labour market
for an explanation. These include search frictions, which
arise because workers and firms sometimes require time to
find a good match with each other. Another at least partial
explanation is that workers resist absolute wage cuts but
accept falls in purchasing power when dollar wages are
constant in the face of positive inflation.
An objection to this line of research is the finding by some BIS

researchers that the plucking pattern is clearer in countries
with more flexible labour markets – mostly English-speaking
ones. The literature focusing on labour market frictions also
does not explain where the originating pluck comes from,
“Downturns are usually short, sharp and
arrive at irregular intervals; expansions are
longer and slower."

### Page 3

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
3
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
only why the data have the dynamics they do when those
shocks occur. Something must be causing the “pluck” shock,
if indeed it is a shock. The BIS paper suggests one possibility:
the authors find that output in the manufacturing and trade
sectors shows a clearer pluck pattern than construction.

Construction downturns tend to linger, perhaps because of
their connections to indebtedness and financial crises.
If we take seriously the patterns in the data showing
asymmetric downturns and the plucking pattern Friedman
described, there are clear policy implications. Currently, the
models underlying central bank forecasts are symmetric by
construction. They therefore systematically understate spare
capacity during expansions. They will find a positive output

gap ahead of a downturn, even when there was no evidence of
overheating at the time. This oversight risks policy mistakes.
The asymmetric nature of downturns also means that the
benefits of macroeconomic stabilisation policy are mostly
about cushioning downturns and hastening recoveries. In
normal times, the benefits will be small.
We also note that the RBA paper mentioned above hypothesised
a short-run NAIRU and a long-run NAIRU that are both pushed

around by actual unemployment in a path-dependent pattern.
This is an argument for “holding on to the employment gains”
that may have been missed by people whose mental model of
the economy is fully symmetric, without any plucking.
The plucking model started from empirical observations
about large downturns. The kind of slow grind upwards in
unemployment seen in Australia in the 2010s and in the past
couple of years does not fit this pattern. It is a qualitatively

different phenomenon requiring other explanations, including
perhaps the path-dependent drag upwards of the feasible
unemployment rate by the actual unemployment rate.
It is these kinds of considerations that underlie our current
forecast for slow growth and not a downturn, even though this
week’s labour market data were concerning. A further 25 basis
points or so of rate increases is unlikely to be the thing that
causes a severe downturn. Typically, downturns are not just

slower expansions but are qualitatively different phases that
require a trigger.
The one thing that makes me nervous is that the plucking
literature finds that every downturn is different. The famous
opening line to Tolstoy’s Anna Karenina, “Happy families are
all alike; every unhappy family is unhappy in its own way,”
would seem to apply to economies as well. And in at least one
case, a forceful tightening of monetary policy was the trigger
for a downturn. The so-called “Volcker disinflation” brought

US inflation under control in the early 1980s. That episode is
widely regarded as a success, despite the output costs. In the
current circumstances, some policymakers and commentators
might be tempted to believe that the same medicine is needed;
nobody wants inflation expectations to become entrenched,
after all. However, as RBA Board member Ian Ross has noted,
the institutions of the 1970s that produced wage–price spirals
are no longer operative. So there is no guarantee that a similar

approach nowadays would be necessary or as effective.
Watch out then, if the discourse turns to arguments that
countries “need a recession” to get inflation under control. A
Volcker-style policy could just end up plucking the economy.
Forecast changes
• We have upgraded our WPI forecast following this week's data
release, with our year-end forecast now 3.4%yr.
• We have also upgraded our near-term unemployment rate,
with a year-end forecast of 4.9%.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
THE WEEK THAT WAS
Cliff Notes: risks mount
risks are skewed to the upside. FOMC members who spoke
throughout the week also kept the focus on price risks, and called
for the FOMC to act swiftly. Governor Waller again proposed a
50bp hike in December; others seemingly favour a 25bp hike at

the December and January meetings, as Westpac forecasts, and
continuing to tighten further in 2027 if necessary.
Very clearly, the labour market and activity growth are not
of concern for US policy makers. Retail sales gave further
justification for this view this week, the control group
surprising to the upside with a 0.6% gain in October after
a material upward revision to September (0.5%). That said,
housing is under considerable pressure, pending home sales

falling 17.1% over the past year, and housing starts back at
2019 levels despite a 9.9% rebound in October. Persistent
weakness in this sector will, in time, have implications for both
consumption and investment.
China’s October data round was the other key international
development of the week. Again, it provided a call for pro-
active stimulus in scale. In the month, annual retail sales
growth almost stalled at 0.4%yr and the decline in fixed
asset investment broadened outside property, with capital

expenditure now down 8.1% across the economy year-to-
date. Industrial production again highlighted that its output to
satiate global demand which is holding up aggregate growth;
though, even here, momentum softened in October, annual
growth in production slowing from 5.5%yr to 4.8%yr. It is not
only activity that remains under pressure, but also wealth,
with new / existing home prices 0.15% / 0.24% lower in the
month. As we continue to emphasise, to prove effective,

stimulus needs to affect change in both activity and wealth
and do so in a sustainable manner.
Australia’s labour market disappointed again in October,
employment falling 43.5k following September’s 36.4k
decline. On a three-month average basis, job gains over the
year have now slowed to 1.1%yr, materially below current
growth in the working-age population, 1.7%yr. As a result, the
unemployment rate rose to a new post-pandemic high of 4.9%
despite the participation rate falling another 0.2ppts to 66.6%.

While employment growth’s descent initially reflected softer
momentum in hiring, now we are seeing evidence of a material
increase in redundancies. Most affected workers look to have
remained in employment to date, but persistent job shedding
raises the risk of a faster and more persistent uptrend in the
unemployment rate, potentially above our baseline peak
forecast of 5.2% (at mid-2027) and most certainly before the
late-2028 peak of 5.1% the RBA expects.

As such, the robust 1.1% (3.5%yr) rise in the Q3 Wage Price
Index warrants close scrutiny. The Q3 increase was skewed
towards those on awards and enterprise arrangements – pay
outcomes directly tied to the Fair Work Commission’s decision
and collective bargaining. Individual arrangements meanwhile,
the most responsive to current and expected labour market
conditions, accounted for their smallest share of a Q3 wage
increase since 2019. Business survey detail also suggests labour

cost growth will normalise quickly after the award increase.
Deteriorating economic conditions are also increasingly evident
in housing. The Westpac Housing Sentiment Index fell 2.2%
to 83.2 in November, among the weakest readings since 2005.
Interestingly, there remains considerable latent demand: almost
13% of consumers intend to purchase a home over the next
year, and relatively few plan to sell. For now, though, weak
sentiment, high interest rates and expectations of further price

declines are keeping would-be buyers on the sidelines. These
factors also pose risks to the broader growth story.
In the US, abstracting from the Anthropic IPO, the outlook for
inflation remained the critical theme. The October FOMC minutes
highlighted that inflation is currently being supported by tariffs
and energy prices as well as the AI buildout, with "several
[members] judg[ing] that price pressures were increasingly
extending beyond industries directly affected by data center

construction”. Participants "assessed that medium- and longer-
term inflation expectations continued to be consistent with the
Committee's 2 percent objective", although persistent above-
target inflation is a threat to the status quo, and perceived
Elliot Clarke, Head of International Economics
Ryan Wells, Economist
Illiana Jain, Economist
“Australia’s labour market delivered a second

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FOCUS ON NEW ZEALAND
Sunny side up
This week we published our final Economic Overview
for 2026. Titled “Sunny side up”, the report shows that
New Zealand’s economic recovery is continuing. Activity
expanded by 2.6% in the year to June, and recent indicators

point to further moderate growth. Overall, GDP is forecast
to grow by 2.8% in both 2027 and 2028. But this remains a
two-speed recovery: export-facing industries and regional
centres are enjoying stronger incomes, while households
and businesses tied to the major metropolitan centres are
still contending with high inflation, a soft labour market and
a subdued housing market.
The global backdrop remains broadly supportive. On an export-
weighted basis, New Zealand’s trading partners are forecast

to grow by 3.1% in 2026, 2.9% in 2027 and 2.8% in 2028.
Stronger growth in the United States and East Asia outside
China, helped by the continuing artificial intelligence investment
buildout, is offsetting some of the drag from higher energy
prices. The central assumption underpinning these forecasts is
that a Middle East agreement eventually restores safe passage
through the Strait of Hormuz, allowing Brent crude to fall
towards US$77 a barrel in late 2027 and US$71 in late 2028.

Against that backdrop, the external sector is the clear bright
spot. Merchandise export volumes are estimated to have risen
by more than 4% this year, with dairy, meat and horticulture
joined by stronger output of manufactured metals, machinery
and equipment. Favourable growing conditions, high world
prices and a low New Zealand dollar have driven this growth.
Services exports are performing even more strongly and
are on track to grow by around 9% this year. Foreign visitor

arrivals continue to recover strongly, with Australians and
Chinese accounting for about two-thirds of the increase
seen this year. In 12-month rolling terms, visitor numbers
are expected to soon move above their 2019 peak, while
other services exports will likely continue their longer-run
outperformance.
Agriculture is expected to remain a major source of strength.
Farmgate returns are expected to stay elevated despite high
energy and shipping costs and an expected modest appreciation

in the New Zealand dollar. Meat export prices should remain
high in real terms over the next couple of years, with beef
supported by firm US demand. Kiwifruit and apple prices are
also expected to remain strong, while log prices should be
broadly stable to slightly firmer in 2027. Dairy prices – at least
powders – appear to have received an additional lift from El
Niño-related drought risk and strong global demand for protein.
We have raised our forecast milk price for the current season

by 50 cents to $10.25 per kilogram of milk solids – still ahead
of Fonterra’s own forecast, which was revised up to a $10.10
midpoint this week. We have also lifted our 2027/28 season
forecast to $9.75, reflecting a more gradual expected rise in the
New Zealand dollar than foreseen previously.
At this point, the strength offshore has not yet translated
into an equally convincing recovery for households. Average
household earnings grew only 2.9% in the year to June, while

high inflation pushed real wage growth into negative territory.
Real per capita household spending was flat over the year, and
weak house prices have limited growth in household wealth.
Spending should improve gradually during 2027 as export
income circulates through the economy, employment recovers
and inflation eases. Even so, the strongest gains are likely to
remain in regions directly exposed to agriculture and tourism.
The labour market has been unusually slow to respond to

improving GDP. Unemployment has climbed to an 11-year high
of 5.7%, and employment growth has failed to keep pace with
population growth. The pattern is best described as “low-
fire, low-hire”: firms avoided the scale of layoffs that might
normally accompany a downturn but may now have enough
staff to accommodate the early stages of a recovery without
immediately stepping up recruitment. Employment appears
to have begun turning higher and hiring intentions have

recovered, but unemployment is unlikely to decline materially
until next year. We expect it to ease to 5.1% by the end of
2027 and 4.5% in 2028.
Housing remains a restraint. National house prices have fallen
about 1.7% this year, sales have weakened and properties are
taking longer to sell. Adjusted for inflation, house prices are on
course for a fourth consecutive annual decline, and we expect
them to fall by around 33% between the 2021 peak and the

end of 2027. That adjustment is improving affordability and
supporting first-home buyers, but it also encourages existing
owners to save rather than spend. As with other economic
indicators, regional differences remain pronounced, with areas
benefiting from agricultural and tourism income outperforming
the major centres.
Residential construction should nevertheless rise during
2027, supported by a pipeline of more than 40,000 consented
homes, much of it medium-density development in Auckland

and Canterbury. The durability of that upswing is uncertain,
however, because financing costs, building costs and weak
house prices remain significant obstacles. Commercial
construction is closer to finding a floor, although office and
retail development is likely to stay subdued for a while given
elevated vacancy rates.
Darren Gibbs
Senior Economist

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
Business investment has been more resilient. Capital equipment
spending rose strongly in the June quarter, and imports point to
further gains despite higher freight costs and a weaker currency.
Confidence rebounded after the initial shock from the Middle

East conflict and has held up despite renewed oil price pressure.
Investment will remain uneven, but cash-rich agricultural
businesses are well placed, and computing equipment and
software are contributing to growth.
Inflation is the main complication. Consumer prices rose 3.7%
in the year to September and annual inflation is expected to
reach 4.3% in December. Higher global oil prices have played
an important role, but the problem is broader than fuel. Core

inflation measures remain around 3%, operating costs are
elevated and firms’ pricing intentions have strengthened.
Headline inflation should fall as energy prices ease and could
temporarily dip below 2% in 2028, but underlying inflation is
expected to decline much more slowly.
That persistence means the RBNZ will likely need to tighten
policy by more than it currently forecasts. The OCR is expected
to reach 3% by year-end, but we think the neutral rate may

be closer to 3.75%, in contrast to the RBNZ’s 3% to 3.5%
estimate. As growth strengthens and core inflation proves
sticky, we expect the OCR to peak at 4% in September 2027
and remain there for about a year. Medium- and long-term
interest rates are therefore likely to settle above the unusually
low levels seen in the decade before the pandemic.
Finally, fiscal conditions should improve as stronger nominal
activity and above-target inflation lift tax revenue. However,

spending pressures and higher debt-servicing costs will be
difficult to avoid. We expect the operating balance before
gains and losses, excluding ACC (OBEGALx), to return to
surplus in 2028/29, while net core Crown debt peaks near
43.7% of GDP in 2027/28. The unresolved election adds
uncertainty, however; the forecasts assume no major near-
term change in policy direction.
Turning to this week’s key economic news, RBNZ Governor
Anna Breman and fellow MPC member Prasanna Gai spoke

to audiences in New Zealand and Australia respectively. We
thought that Breman’s comments were reasonably balanced.
On the hawkish side, Breman noted that some indicators of
economic activity had been stronger than expected. Perhaps
of greater note, she also said that this month’s partial October
CPI data (discussed below) indicated that indirect impacts
from the fuel price shock might be growing following a more
muted than expected impact in the September quarter. On

the dovish side, she noted subdued domestic demand, which
she said was making it difficult for firms to pass on higher
costs. She also referred to weak labour market data (including
weak wage growth) and the stability seen in medium-term
inflation expectations (also discussed below). Following the
October review Breman had commented that the chances of a
December rate hike were “high”.
In our view, Gai’s commentary leaned more on the dovish
side. He reiterated his concerns about the implications for NZ

of a bursting of the AI bubble and described recent inflation
and inflation expectations data as “quiescent”. In response to
a question, he indicated that he considered that news since
the October meeting had validated his decision to vote for a
pause at that meeting. The general tone of Gai’s commentary
suggested it would not be a surprise if he voted to extend the
pause at next month’s meeting. Even if he were to vote for
a hike, it seems likely that Gai would regard this as simply a

tentative step towards the roughly 3.25% neutral rate that the
Bank projected in the September Monetary Policy Statement.
On the data front, the partial CPI data for October pointed
to weaker than expected food price inflation but firmer than
expected increases in travel costs. The latter was probably
responsible for Breman’s comments above, but given the
month-to-month volatility of this data we would be hesitant
to read too much into one month’s outcome. These opposing

impacts netted out and so the outcome was consistent
with our forecast for a lift in annual inflation to 4.3% in the
December quarter. Meanwhile, the RBNZ’s long-running
survey of professional forecasters reported a lift in year-ahead
inflation expectations. However, encouragingly, the closely
watched 2-year-ahead measure was little changed at 2.36%.
Moreover, 5-year-ahead and 10-year-ahead expectations –
which had been singled out for special attention by RBNZ

Chief Economist Conway in a recent speech – fell to 2.17%
and 2.13% respectively. In both cases, these were the
lowest readings in almost two years. Finally, encouragingly,
the BusinessNZ services PMI continued its gradual trend
improvement in October, rising to a 3-year high of 51.7.

### Page 7

We expect this positive momentum to have carried into Q3 
2026, with further solid gains in residential building, private 
non-residential building and private engineering construction, 
particularly electricity-related projects. Public infrastructure 
investment will offset some of this strength, with the drag that 
began last year continuing as major projects reach completion.
AUS: October CPI (%mth)
November 25, Last: 0.8%, Westpac f/c: 0.4%  
Mkt f/c: 0.4%, Range : 0.3 to 0.6
We expect the October CPI to rise 0.41%mth with the annual 
pace lifting from 4.3%yr to 4.8%yr, the fastest pace in the 
short history. Westpac expects this to be the peak in current 
acceleration in annual inflation. Our seasonally adjusted CPI 
estimate lifts 0.7%mth, a small step up from the 0.6% increase 
in September, highighting the seasonal softness in the month. 
Transport makes the largest contribution (+0.35ppts), almost 
entirely due to auto fuel, with smaller contributions from 
clothing & footwear (+0.06ppts), household goods & services 
(+0.05ppts) and meals out & takeaway (+0.04ppts). The 
main offsets are recreation & culture (–0.18ppts) with fruit & 
vegetables, utilities and others providing marginal offsets.
For more details please see “October CPI Preview“.  
AUS: October Trimmed Mean (%mth)
November 25, Last: 0.2%, Westpac f/c: 0.4% 
Mkt f/c: 0.4%, Range : 0.3 to 0.5
Our October Trimmed Mean estimate is 0.42%mth, a step up from 
0.3% in September, taking the annual pace to 3.7%yr from 3.6%yr 
and lifting the six month annualised pace to 4.2%yr, from 4.0%yr, 
which is also the fastest pace in the short history of the series. 
Rents and dwellings continue to underpin the TM estimates. 
We see risks as slightly tilted to the upside. Our estimates 
allow for a greater degree of price resetting in areas such as 
meals out & takeaway and personal and household services, 
but this adjustment proves broader than our assumptions. 
Fuel levy surcharges are another source of upside risk, 
including increases by Woolworths and Australia Post. The 
key downside risk remains housing, particularly new dwelling 
prices, which surprised to the downside in recent months.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
NZ: Q3 retail spending (%qtr)
Nov 23, Last: -0.5, Westpac: 1.4
The June quarter saw a larger than expected 0.5% fall in
retail spending, with high fuel prices squeezing households’
spending power. However, even with the drag from high
fuel prices, core spending (excl. fuels and vehicles) remained

resilient, rising by 0.7%. With fuel prices dropping through
much of the September quarter, we expect overall spending
levels will lift by 1.4%. Importantly, with lower fuel prices
putting money back into households’ pockets, that’s likely to
be underpinned by firm gains in core spending categories.
AUS: Q3 Private CAPEX (%qtr)
Nov 26, Last: –3.6%, WBC f/c: 10.0%
Mkt f/c: 4.5%, Range: 2.0% to 10.0%
Private capex declined 3.6%qtr in Q2 2026 as data centre-

related machinery and equipment investment pulled back
sharply. With nominal ADP equipment imports surging
83%qtr to another record high, we expect a sizeable rebound
in the Q3 figures. Buildings and structures should also find
support as the broader strength in private non-residential
approvals through the first half of 2026 works through the
pipeline. Overall, we expect private capex to grow a strong
10.0%qtr in Q3, with data centres again leading the charge.

Est 3 for 2026/27 capex plans, after our adjustment, is
consistent with impressive nominal growth of 17.9%yr and
real growth of 15.3%yr. We expect Est 4 unadjusted plans for
2026/27 to come in at $221bn, a 15.5% increase on a year ago,
indicative of the scale of investment yet to occur.

2 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Consumers Feel One Way, but Spend Another
Kirsten Cabacungan, Vice President and Senior Investment Strategist
Americans have spent much of the past several years telling surveys they are cautious
about the economy. Yet they continue to spend as though they are not. The University
of Michigan’s Consumer Sentiment Index remains near multidecade lows, while the
Conference Board’s Index of Consumer Confidence has also been in a yearslong downtrend.

These surveys reflect a list of persistent concerns from elevated food and gas prices to
geopolitical uncertainty and labor market anxiety.
Even so, consumer spending has remained remarkably resilient. The final estimate of Q2
real consumer spending, as measured by PCE, was revised up to an annualized rate of
3.8%, well above the 2.3% 20-year average. More recent data suggest that momentum
has continued. According to Bank of America Institute, the latest monthly total credit and

debit card spending data per household rose 6.2% year-over-year (YoY) in September, and
spending excluding gasoline was up 5.1% YoY, both the third strongest growth rates in the
past four years.1
The persistence of consumer spending is particularly notable given that temporary cyclical
boosts like larger tax refunds are starting to fade. That raises an important question: What is
actually funding strong consumption today? Recent data suggest the drivers are becoming

increasingly more diversified, with broadening labor market trends, healthy consumer
finances and elevated wealth effects all reinforcing one another and underpinning consumers.
First, the labor market continues to provide an important foundation for consumer spending,
and recent trends suggest wage growth is supporting a broader set of households than in
recent years. Jobs growth has slowed from the exceptionally strong pace seen following the
pandemic, but broader indicators suggest labor market conditions remain relatively stable.

Although the September jobs report came in softer than expected, the three- and six-month
rolling averages indicate that the trend in jobs growth likely bottomed out last year and has
gradually improved since (Exhibit 1A). Meanwhile, the unemployment rate remains low by
historical standards and layoffs remain contained. And while wage growth has moderated
and recently trailed headline inflation (Exhibit 1B), cumulative income gains over the past
several years have likely helped many households absorb higher prices better.

An important development though has been improving wage growth trends among lower-
income households, where spending tends to be most sensitive to changes in wages. Bank
of America Institute consumer deposit account data show after-tax wage growth for lower-
income households outpaced higher-income households for a second consecutive month
in August, rising 4.7% YoY compared with 3.5% for higher-income households. Importantly,
this improvement appears to be supporting discretionary spending, not just spending

on necessities. Lower-income discretionary spending rose 5.7% YoY in August, nearly
matching the 5.9% pace for higher-income households. In other words, the narrowing gap
in wage and spending growth across income cohorts suggests consumer resilience looks
to be becoming less dependent on affluent households alone, creating a broader and more
durable foundation for consumption.
Second, household finances remain in relatively good shape, providing consumers with the

flexibility to continue spending. The ratio of household debt to disposable personal income
remains near its lowest level since the late 1990s, excluding the temporary distortions
caused by pandemic-related income support in 2020 and 2021 (Exhibit 1C). Recent
revisions to the Bureau of Economic Analysis national income accounts also suggest
consumers have a larger financial buffer than previous data suggested. Personal saving
estimates over the past year have been revised higher by $320 billion, or 39%, and the

revised savings rate remains above 4% after previous estimates showed it fell to 3% during
the summer.2 Concerns that consumers may be rapidly exhausting their savings buffers
may therefore be overstated. Supporting this view, Bank of America Institute consumer
1 Bank of America Institute, “Consumer Checkpoint: Strength in numbers.” As of October 9, 2026.
2 Bank of America, “Massive Savings Markup = Another Reason to Be Bullish.” As of October 2, 2026.

Investment Implications
Consumer spending strength
remains a key pillar of economic
and earnings growth, which keeps
us constructive on our Equity
overweight. The growing connection
between household wealth and
spending reinforces the importance
of maintaining a diversified portfolio.

### Page 3

3 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
deposit account data show that middle- and lower-income households continue to maintain
deposit balances above prepandemic levels across age groups.
Third, elevated household wealth continues to provide a powerful tailwind for consumer
spending, particularly among households that account for an outsized share of overall
consumption. U.S. household net worth rose $12.8 trillion (T) to a record $195.9T in Q2,

driven largely by gains in Equities, which contributed $10.7T of the increase (Exhibit 1D).
As a result, household net worth relative to disposable income climbed to a record 8.3,
meaning households now hold more than eight dollars of wealth for every dollar of annual
disposable income. Households are also sitting on $35.8T in home equity, an all-time high
level that provides a significant balance sheet cushion. Rising asset values have helped to

support spending through what economists refer to as wealth effects. In other words, when
households feel wealthier, they tend to be more willing to spend.
For investors, the key takeaway is that consumer spending is being supported by multiple
engines despite the weaker sentiment data. Labor income trends are supporting wider
participation, healthy balance sheets are helping households absorb economic pressures,
and elevated household wealth continues to sustain higher-income spending. Together,

these forces are supportive of economic growth and corporate profits, which is particularly
important since the consumer accounts for roughly 70% of U.S. gross domestic product.
That said, it does not eliminate risk. A meaningful risk to the consumer would be a scenario
in which both the labor and wealth engines weaken simultaneously. For now, labor market
conditions remain stable, and wealth effects remain supportive. Equity market pullbacks in
recent years have done little to derail consumer spending, suggesting it would likely take a

deeper and more prolonged drawdown to alter consumer behavior.
